  has no source; the eye candy's source is not passed to the burn), the source's stat 16 bonus, the notified AI pass of a surviving NPC, the burner as the attacker for the difficulty rule, the item-script events with the burner as attacker, and fire eye candy owned by an object the viewer knows no tile of.

### 3.6 The hero (TM14, TM11, GQ5 A; S10–S13)

**The hero is an entry like any NPC** (the player's entity), and **its actions are runs of the table**. Its location is written at every step (RL5), which retires the commit points of S4 and the
viewer-owned grid position as the truth of the hero's cell (GR33); the session answers "where is the hero" at every moment.

**The request boundary (GQ5 A).** `Arcanum.World` gets one typed request per player action, applied at once in single player, with no queue, returning a typed result: `WalkRequest` (tile, run), `ObjectClickRequest`
(target, Shift, Alt, the cursor mode from the interface stack), `ThrowRequest`, `UseItemRequest`, `SkillRequest`, `CastRequest`, `StopRequest` (the right button's release: interrupt at level 3 and turn toward the cursor)
and `FaceRequest`. The viewer turns clicks, keys and the stick into requests and draws; the rules inside change state directly, as the original's single-player branch does (SA34). The request set grows with
each slice; the ones outside goals (item moves, combat mode, raising a stat, follower orders) belong to later work. The pure rules are `HeroClickRules` (the decision table of GS26, the start branch of GS27 including
the auto-attack option, the round's "only while idle" rule and the run-flag setters) and the always-run rule (GS25), in `Arcanum.Simulation.Goals` (pure rules; `Arcanum.World` applies them).

**The hero's goals.** The tile click is goal 3 or 4 (retarget in place, GS14); the object click builds the goal itself, with no start gate: attack 18 or 19, talk 21, use 38 and 81, pickup 15, throw 37, the trap walk
(GS26). Talk, use and loot are goals whose **last node acts** through a port: `OpenDialog(target)` (script point 9 then the dialog), `OpenLoot`, `FireUseScript` (point 1), the door goals of §3.5. The five after-route
intents of the viewer disappear: the approach is the goal's own node 8, an interrupt (a stun, a new click, a window that takes the hero's goal along as the original's windows do, S7 of MIG12) ends the run and the intent
with it. Spell, skill and item cursors start the shared starters (the casting run, the skill starter, the item-use starters); the hero's `AttackGoal` (S12a), the held throw (S12b) and `SpellCastGoal` (S12c) became holders of the runs they name.

**Walking.** The hero uses the NPC walk goal and the same mover (`AreaAiMovers`), so a stun or knock-down can stop the hero as it stops an NPC once S13 starts the hero's stun on the table (B11; the hold itself is S13b's read, GT145–GT153). The viewer's grid actor and its movement time (C3) leave the hero's rule
path; the stick and the gamepad become a held `WalkRequest` (ARC-DEV-147, S10c: the original's held button repeats the walk every 250 ms and marks the run as a flight, [the S10c read](#the-s10c-read-of-the-executable), Q7). The hero's gait, smoothing and the stick's feel are presentation of the same steps (risk 3). ARC-DEV-146 (S10b, first part) puts the walk and the turn on the table as runs of the owner `pc` with the route follower and the grid actor as their feet; the stepper's own steps follow in S10b-2 (goal 53 in S10b-2a, the walk nodes in S10b-2b, the location in S10b-2c).

**In a round.** The click is taken only on the hero's turn and only while the hero is idle; the hero passes the start gate (it tests only NPCs), is checked by the movement gate and pays AP in the round host as now
(GS15, GS27, TL44). The round host (G6) stays as the turn machine and reads the table for the wait on animations (§3.7).

**A session without a hero (viewer only).** The original always has a hero, so the movement gate (0x4339A0) always reads one. The viewer also runs a diagnostic PC profile without `--hero`, and its synthetic
fixtures have no hero either; there the session has no life to read, and the gate treats the actor as able to act unless it lies down (ARC-DEV-164, `HeroActing`). Before it the table refused every click walk of such a
session (found by the full smoke of 2026-11-06, F3). A session with a hero reads its life as before.

### 3.7 Rounds on the table (S5a: the sweeps; S5b, ARC-DEV-136: the rest)

**S5a landed the sweeps:** round start interrupts every run at level 3 through the session (`SweepGoalsForRoundStart`, the current goals that are "not counted" are left), each participant again, and the turn set interrupts the
previous participant unless it is the hero (GS19, GS20, TL36, TL37); the host runs the end nodes of what ended.

**S5b (ARC-DEV-136) landed the rest.**

- **The callback.** The table counts the significant goals (priority ≥ 2, no no-interrupt flag, not "not counted", TL39; derived from the runs, §4) and the session calls the round's "all animations done" callback
  (`GameSessionOwner.AnimationsDone`, 0x4B7080, installed at the round start through 0x423FE0) after **every free** that leaves the count at zero (0x44CF20, GT11): a stepper's end, a start's interrupts, an
  interrupt, a sweep, a kill, a map's close, a departure, a lost step; one call for each ended run whose free saw no significant goal left, so a sweep that frees three runs calls it up to three times, as
  the original's frees do. The callback does what TH5 and TL39 read: a PC-type object with action points left keeps its subturn and nothing follows (the session also requires the hero to be active, §4); otherwise the stall detector sees the current participant with its action
  points (the same participant at two callbacks in a row with unchanged action points sets the forced flag, with A11 of the Original mode the reference of a new participant being the previous one's points) and only
  then is the turn-based event (kind 12, `combat.step`, 2 ms of real time ahead, one pending) asked for, so the detector is fed even when an event is already pending (GT24). Nothing is saved: no revision moves. This replaces the round host's `CompleteCombatActions` twice-unchanged
  rule, which asked for the event every frame; the stall detector itself stays, as TL39 describes it.
- **The handler.** `CompleteCombatActions` is the event's handler (0x4B7150): an active hero with points keeps its turn; a participant the callbacks marked as stalled spends the rest of its points; one without
  points passes the turn. It observes nothing and **tests no animation** (GT23): an NPC whose own run is still playing is passed or has its rest spent exactly as one without a run (TH5: an action it cannot pay is cut by the
  next turn set's interrupt). The round host (`AreaTurnBased.Step`) is the handler's host when the event falls due in the frame step (MIG12 S8), so it still fires behind the Esc menu; the host's
  per-frame call (`AreaTurnBased.Frame`) follows the round and plays the NPCs' subturn hooks and no longer asks for the event. The one wait left is the hero's viewer-hosted action (§4). The stalled flag is global in the original; the Steamwoven setting spends it only on the participant it was set for (§4).
- **The round start holds the callback.** The sweep and the participants' interrupts come before the callback is registered and the round flag set (TH10), so the host wraps them in
  `SuppressRoundCallback()`; a turn set's interrupt of the previous participant's runs is inside the round, and its last free calls the callback for the new participant, before its hook (TH10 puts it before the new participant's
  action points are set; the session grants them at the turn set, which changes nothing under A11, where only the handle is stored, and is a small difference under the Steamwoven setting).
- **TL41.** A run started in a round for an owner that is not the current participant (the hero's turn, another critter's, a critter outside the round) gets its first frame 1 ms of animation time after the start
  (`RoundFirstGoalStepDelayMs`, GT17) instead of 5 ms. The gated starters (the walks and runs through the movement gate 0x4339A0; follow, attack, throw and spread out through the start gate 0x4348E0) refuse a critter that is
  not current: the executor's starters ran that test with the action-point rule already, and the table's start gate (`AreaAiHost.StartGatePasses`) is the second line for every kind of those six; the hit reactions, dying,
  knockback and the goals scripts or the combat code push pass the gate and get the 1 ms frame.
- **The executor reads every run.** The equal-goal and alias tests of the attack, follow and throw starters walk every run of the NPC (`IAiGoalWorld.RunsOf`, the original's 0x44E710), not its first; the host starts no
  overlay of its own, so this changes nothing visible until S6–S9 start them.
- **The subturn hook after a stepper's end stays the round host's next frame** (`FidgetAfterHook`), as before: the callback is the free's, the hook is the host's. The hero's actions are no runs yet (S10–S13), so an idle hero
  without points and with no significant goal playing stands for the free of its last slot and the host calls the callback for it once (`AreaTurnBased.Frame`); an NPC's pass re-asks for the event while the hero's
  viewer-hosted action plays. S10–S13 retire both bridges.

### 3.8 Scripts (S14)

The script effects that start goals (perform animation 62, walk to 69, run to 74, get up 76, use item 79, steal 115, the casts 48, 61, 118, 121, 122, 136, kill 58 through the death path and dying 31, the two eye-candy actions) become requests applied by
the world runtime with the starters of §3.3 and their own gates (62 and the casts have none); stop attacking 109 (level 3) and become prone 93 (level 4, no goal) are interrupts. No script action calls the attack,
follow, flee, throw, pickup, wander or fidget starters (GS30). GR27 is closed by S14g (ARC-DEV-243: the damage actions 60 and 138 and heal hp 75); the casts were S14f.

**S14a (ARC-DEV-231, done):** [the S14 read](#the-s14-read-of-the-executable) (GT154–GT167). Walk to (69) and run to (74) are `ScrObjectStateRequest`s (`WalkTo`, `RunTo`, the tile in `Value`/`Value2`; admission set S25, in the placed-script and heartbeat sets) that the host hands to `AreaAiHost.ScriptWalk`:
the movement gate, an existing walk (the finder for goal 4 with its aliases) retargeted in place, else goal 7 (or goal 55, now interpreted with goal 7's nodes) at level 2 through the generic starter; the run turns the run flag on below encumbrance 4, also on a found walk of the same tile (GT161).
**S14b (ARC-DEV-233, done):** heal fatigue (76, GT162) is a request per object (`HealFatigue`, the amount in `Value`) that the viewer applies through the session's life step (`LifeHeal.Fatigue`), whose critter-state starter already starts the get-up of a critter that comes round; a negative amount (it raises the damage in the original) is not modelled. Perform animation (62, GT155–GT157) is a request (`PerformAnimation`) that `AreaAiHost.ScriptAnimate` turns into goal 0 (the animation once from frame 0, [the S14b read](#the-s14b-read-of-the-executable) GT168–GT172) or, for animation 10 of a critter, goal 60 (kneel, hands once, then goal 51 kneeling back; GT175–GT178), through the generic starter at priority 3 with no gate (a round included), all three interpreted in `ExecutorAnimateGoals.cs` over the stun's ports. A number outside 0..25 starts nothing. Set S26 holds both actions. A target that is not an NPC of the shown map (the PC, another map's object, a non-critter) is left as it is and the run's other effects apply. Not modelled: the art-type test of GT173 (every NPC is a critter); the viewer reads and draws only the animations 7, 8 and 17..19 (`NpcAnimationClip`), so any other animation has one frame and its goal pops at the first step, goal 60's sequence included; the stunned bit 0x20 of the guards (the ports read 0x40 only); the hold of animation 14 (0xDB flag 0x4) and the destroyed-object flag of the step (GT170, GT179); the fall-through of a refused goal 60 to goal 0 (GT156); the refused start's 2 action points go to the owner (the stun's pattern), not the current participant (GT180); the reverse step's guard is the project's (GT178 names the start's only); the start resets to frame 0 (GT169 keeps the frame bits). Followers can be animated as any NPC.

**S14c (ARC-DEV-234, done; no new read, GT163–GT164 of the S14 read):** try to steal 100 coins (115) is a request (`StealCoins`: the thief in `Object`, the victim in `Other`; admission set S27) that `AreaAiHost.ScriptSteal` applies: nothing happens unless the victim (an NPC of the shown map, or the PC) holds at least 100 coins (`QueryNpcGold`, `QueryGold`); then the skill starter `StartUseSkill` (the start gate with the skill cost, goal 33 with skill 5, run flag 0x4000) starts the thief's pickpocket run. **Not ready (closed by S14l, ARC-DEV-246):** the NPC pickpocket attempt of goals 33 → 86 transferred nothing in the AI host (it recorded a Skill notice); it now takes min(coins, 100) through the session; the stack of coins (GT164's "whole stack") is still not passed on as the goal's item (the attempt reads the victim's pile); a thief that is the PC is not modelled (the hero's pickpocket is the window's); a follower or companion thief is not refused (GT164 checks no type); a thief already on that victim keeps its run (a heartbeat repeats the action every beat). Use item with skill (79) was already done by ARC-SKILLS-009 (SX3, SX4) and needs no change.

**S14d (ARC-DEV-235, done, docs only):** [the S14d read](#the-s14d-read-of-the-executable) (GT183–GT193) refines GS30 (within three calls of the goal builders and interrupt functions; the actions four calls deep, GT193, are not read): kill 58 is already a death with no killer through the session's kill (the kill starts dying, GT185), 109 stop attacking is `ApplyStop` (GT188), 120 play script eye candy is an existing request (`ScrEffects.EyeCandies`, admitted for item-use scripts) that no area host plays yet, and 93 become prone (GT187, S14e) starts no goal. **The six cast actions (48, 61, 118, 121, 122, 136) are not implemented:** a script cast is the spell dispatcher without the UI's cost, line-of-fire and reach checks (`SessionSpellCaster.CastAt` pays the cost of a critter source and tests the line of fire, and the casting goal's start charges the pre-animation cost unless the run is free; there is no reach check), with the free (0x2) and unresistable (0x4) invocation flags, and 61 / 136 / a null caster have no parent and no casting goal (GT184); that needs a spell-caster entry point, the viewer's cast host and tests of its own, so it is the follow-up slice S14f for the coordinator to give a code. 36 attack starts goal 53 or 0 for an NPC victim (GT190, low confidence), which GS30 and the line above ("no script action calls the attack starters") did not say; the interrupts 21, 22, 67, 68, 23, 24 and 86 (GT192) are also unimplemented. The damage actions 60 and 138 (GT186) and heal hp 75 are not in the interpreter either (follow-up with the casts).

**S14e (ARC-DEV-236, done):** become prone (93, GT187) is a `BecomeProne` request per object of its operand (admission set S28) that `AreaAiHost.ScriptProne` applies to an NPC of the shown map: the interrupt of the NPC's runs at level 4 (`InterruptOwner`; a refusal by a priority-5 run, dying, does nothing), then the art becomes the fall (animation 7) at its last frame; no goal starts and no flag, combat mode, focus or follower changes. The NPC then lies with no run until something starts its get-up (a goal started on it meets the fall pose and pushes the get-up, GT7). A companion can be laid down too (GT187 has no follower exclusion). Open limits: the pose is not saved (a load or map change stands the NPC again, as for the knock-down), a dead NPC with no dying run has its death pose replaced by the plain fall (GT187 has no type test), and a destroyed NPC is not guarded. Stop attacking (109, GT188) was already `ApplyStop` (level 3, ARC-SCRIPT-052).

**S14f (ARC-DEV-241, done):** the six cast actions (48, 61, 118, 121, 122, 136, GT183/GT184, admission set S29) are one `CastSpell` request (`ScrObjectStateRequest`: the target in `Object`, the caster in `Other` or none, the spell in `Value`, the invocation flags `ScrCastFlags.Free` 0x2 / `Unresistable` 0x4 in `Value2`); the run goes on (GT154). The cast host's script entry point `SessionSpellCaster.CastByScript` is perform's cast without an item: the source and parent are the caster (none for 61, 136 and an absent caster handle, so no parent, no casting goal and the run begins by its event), the invocation check (0x4564E0) and the run with the Free and Unresistable run flags. It makes none of the spell UI's own tests: not the cost (spell.mes 600) and not the line-of-fire message (spell.mes 604); the line of fire itself stays inside the invocation check (0x4564E0 calls 0x456BC0), so a blocked line refuses a script cast silently, which refines the "without the UI's cost, line-of-fire and reach checks" of the S14d text. `AreaAiHost.ScriptCast` starts an NPC caster's casting goal (26 or 82) as perform's cast does (queued under a running cast, refused at depth 4 or in a round, the generic starter's interrupt) and `AreaUseScriptHost` applies the request for a target that is an NPC of the shown map or the PC. **NOT READY:** the damage actions 60 and 138 (GT186) and heal hp 75 (GT190) are not in the interpreter yet (S14g, code from the coordinator). Smoke `ai-cast` gained a leg (a free, unresistable script cast by the NPC at the hero plays the NPC's casting goal and begins its run; a cast with no caster starts a parentless run that begins by its event).

**S14g (ARC-DEV-243, done):** [the S14g read](#the-s14d-read-of-the-executable) (GT194–GT197) turns damage (60), apply unresistable damage (138) and heal hit points (75) (set S30) into one `Damage` / `DamageUnresistable` / `HealHitPoints` request per object of the operand, in list order (amount in `Value`, the damage type in `Value2`). `SessionSpellCaster.ApplyScriptDamage` is the burn's path with the amount in the type's slot: the damage step with no attacker (resistance, invulnerability, walls; **no armour, no difficulty scaling, no critical effect**), the resistance skipped by the unresistable flag only, applied to the critter's life (a death with no killer, a knock-down only from fatigue damage through the life step) or through the map to an object; the viewer applies it to the PC and to an NPC of the shown map (a killed NPC falls with its death pose), and heals hit points through the session's heal (no resurrection, no get-up). A type outside 0..4 and an amount of 0 or less for the heal do nothing (project choices where the original overwrites context fields). **NOT READY:** the target's script at point 0x17 that can cancel the damage (ARC-DEV-254, done in S14j), the floaters, damage to a door or container (ARC-DEV-255; the request is left as it is for any object but the PC and an NPC), the reaction-mood update after a heal (0x4B80E0), and the NPC's immediate AI pass after surviving damage. A destroyed NPC takes none (flags 0x400001); an amount past ±100 000 is cut to it (the damage step's limit). Smoke `ai-cast` gained a leg (a script's damage, unresistable fire damage and heal on the NPC change its hit points as read). GR27 closes with this slice.

**S14h (ARC-DEV-244, done):** a script's cast whose caster is the PC (48, 118, 121, 122 with the PC as the first object) goes to the viewer with the PC's scheduler id as the caster. The viewer starts the run through the cast host's script entry point (`CastByScript` with the hero as source and parent: no cost test, the Free and Unresistable run flags, the invocation check silent on a refusal) and then plays the hero's own casting goal 26 as a click's cast does: queued under a playing cast, refused at depth 4 or in a round, otherwise the run's goal on the table (the generic starter interrupts the walk; `CastRequest`). A hero who is down casts nothing (a project choice: the original makes no such test). Limits: the casting goal's start still charges its action points in a round and can refuse with its visible message (only the invocation check is silent); a cast made from an NPC's heartbeat during a round is judged by the round's actor, and a heartbeat that repeats the cast restarts the hero's goal every beat, as the script asks (the smoke calls the entry point outside an AI pass). No new executable read (the S14f read, GT183/GT184, already covers a caster handle of the PC). Smoke `cast` gained a leg (a free script cast by the PC at an NPC plays the casting goal, pays no fatigue at its BEGIN and frees the instant run).

**S14i (ARC-DEV-245, done):** two of the critter-side steps GT195 lists for a script's damage. (1) Fire damage above 0 after resistance (`AttackDamage.DamagesArmor`) wears the critter's torso armour by 10: `GameSessionOwner.ApplyArmorWear(expected, SessionWearSide)` is the attack wear's armour step with no attacker (`ValidSideItems` is the attack wear's item validation, shared; a wearer with no items known or no armour is a NoChange, an NPC's broken item is no longer worn, a first break takes the hero's off its slot); the viewer passes the hero's or the NPC's worn items. (2) A follower of the PC that falls from 20 % or more to below it, by hit points (not a kill, FO16), gets the near-death comment `FollowerRules.NearDeathOfHit` (lines 5500–5599 toward its PC leader, 0x4AEAB0); like the other loyalty lines it is logged and shown in the answer's text, no host speaks it yet. **NOT READY:** the floater of the line (setting 2), the reaction-mood update after a heal, and the other damage paths (attack, spell, burn, trap) that do not call these two yet: the armour wear is the attack's own and the spell executor's duty list still names it. Hand-over notes for the next two tasks (found while scoping S14i). **ARC-DEV-254 (taking-damage script, point 0x17), done in S14j for the script damage only; the rest of this note is the plan for the other paths:** every damage path would call the hook before its damage step (`SessionSpellCaster.ApplyScriptDamage`, `ApplyBurn`, the spell executor's damage component, the attack and trap paths; GT96 and GT195 describe the original: the victim's attached script runs with the damager as triggerer, a skip-default answer vetoes the damage, re-entry is guarded); `AreaUseScriptHost.RunHeartbeat` is the model of a point run for an NPC. **ARC-DEV-255 (door and container damage):** `AreaSpellCastMap.ObjectCombat` returns null and `DamageObject` false, and every damage path (script, burn, spell, trap) goes through those two, so no door or container loses anything anywhere; the original (GT195) takes a material value off a portal or container and destroys it at 0 hit points, the trap check first. It needs a hit-point model for those objects (state by identity in the object directory, hence a save schema step and a `GameSessionSnapshotUpgrade` step), the destroy path (0x43F1C0, not read) and the door and container hosts reading it.

**S14j (ARC-DEV-254, done):** [the S14j read](#the-s14d-read-of-the-executable) (GT198–GT203) gives the taking-damage script (attachment point 0x17, new `ScrPoint.TakingDamage`) to a script's damage (60/138). `SessionSpellCaster.TakingDamage` (host delegate, victim and attacker in, whether the damage goes on out) is asked at the top of `ApplyScriptDamage`, after the type and object checks and before every gate or step (`SessionScriptHit.Vetoed` says it cancelled). The viewer answers it with `AreaUseScriptHost.RunTakingDamage` (any object of the shown map that has a slot at the point, from line 0, triggerer = the attacker or the null handle, under the heartbeat admission set, the slot's flags and counters kept as map changes `scr23.*`; the guard `_inTakingDamage` brackets the whole run including the effects it applies, so a damage the script deals goes on unguarded by itself, GT200) and `TakingDamageVetoes` (the dispatcher's skip default). A script number whose file the content lacks, a trap script and a script that runs past its end skip the default (the veto, as GT199); a run the viewer cannot apply (admission refusal, effects it cannot apply, an unreadable or changed file, a requested teleport) cancels nothing, and a dialog the script asks for is not started. The PC has no script data here, so its damage goes on. **NOT READY:** the script damage only asks it for a living NPC of the shown map and the PC (`AreaUseScriptHost`'s apply path gates a destroyed NPC, a door or a container out first; doors and containers are ARC-DEV-255), where the original runs it for every target (GT198); the other damage paths (attack, spell damage component, burn, trap) do not ask it yet (the hook's shape, victim and attacker, fits them: the burn's attacker is the burning object, GT94; the attack paths pass theirs); the extra object of the worn-item points 34/35 (GT201); the floater and the experience steps are after the veto in the original and are not part of the script damage.

**S14k (ARC-DEV-255, done):** damage to a door, window, container, scenery or trap (GT195: "a non-critter loses a material value and is destroyed at 0 hit points, trap check first") needed no new read and no schema step: ARC-COMBAT-008 already holds the object rules (OD1–OD12, F2) and their state (`object.damage`, `object.condition` map changes). The no-attacker apply is `GameSessionOwner.DamageObject` (the damage step's hit-point total added to the object's damage, the break at 0) with `QueryObjectCombat` giving the damage step the object's material and clamped resistances (a portal's or container's material reduction, none for scenery and traps); `AreaSpellCastMap` answers the cast host's `ObjectCombat` / `DamageObject` for the shown map's doors, windows, containers, scenery and traps, so a spell, a burn, a trap and (S14g's actions 60 / 138) a script's damage reach them; the script host admits them for a script's damage. A static row of a sector now has the cast id `sector:S:N`. **NOT READY:** the trap run at the break (reported as `TrapFires`, ordered before the break in the state but run by no host, as for the hero's attack) and the BUST script, the object's script at point 0x17 for a spell's, a burn's and a trap's damage (a script's damage asks it through S14j's hook), the witnesses, damage to items, objects of another map. Smoke `ai-cast` gained a leg (a script's damage breaks a scenery of the map).
**S14l (ARC-DEV-246, done):** [the S14l read](#the-s14d-read-of-the-executable) (GT204–GT209) gives the NPC thief's pick pocket (skill 5 of goal 86, started by script action 115, S14c) its attempt. `GameSessionOwner.PickPocketByNpc` (`SessionNpcPickPocket`, `SessionNpcPickPocketResult`) runs the shared check with the NPC's own pick-pocket level and training opposed by the victim's perception (no fate point, no difficulty-setting term: both the local PC's) and, on a success, moves min(coins, 100) of the victim's gold to the thief (`GiveGoldToNpc` from the PC, `MoveGoldBetweenNpcs` from an NPC); a failure is noticed per `PickPocketRules.Detection`. `AreaAiHost.AttemptSkill` for skill 5 is `PickPocketAttempt`: it measures the victim (perception, state, unaware of the thief) through the host's critter view, answers the check's success (run flag 0x40000) and, for a noticed failure, runs the victim's catching-thief script (point 11) with the NPC thief as the triggerer (`AreaAiScene.CatchingThief` → `AreaUseScriptHost.RunCatchingThief(row, rules, thief)`; a skip default or a run the viewer cannot finish stops there, as the hero's) and then the attack event of the thief on the victim (loudness 1). A thief on itself, a victim with no gold or a gold kind that is no gold is refused before anything is rolled. **NOT READY:** the thief's own caught-thief script (point 33), the thief's alignment change and the victim's "item stolen" mark (the session keeps no NPC alignment or critter flags 2), the NPC-leader stop (only the PC leads in the host), the gold pile's art size (1 × 1) and the insert check for a thief with no pile (accepted), the thief's action points (the goal's node pays 4; the failure's 2 more of node 9 are not charged), steal of an item other than gold or a plant by an NPC (no caller exists: GT205). Smoke `ai` still reaches the attempt through the thief-on-itself case (refused).

**S14m (ARC-DEV-258, done; no new read, GT63 and the PF/TW rows):** the AI's grenade step (SP14, `AiGroundRules.Grenade`) starts the throw goal 37 with a generic item (type 14, the grenade flag) at the target's tile, and `ThrowByNpc` used to refuse it (only the wielded "default throws" weapon was thrown). `ThrowByNpc` now sends a generic item of the NPC's list to `ThrowNpcGeneric`: the item leaves the NPC and lies hidden on the thrower's cell (`LandNpcItem`, a revision of its own), and an NPC shot (`_npcShots`, separate from the hero's `PendingShot` table) carries it with the thrown range (`ThrowRules.Range` of its weight under the thrower's strength and throwing training). `AreaAiHost.ThrowItem` hands each shot to the scene (`AreaAiScene.LaunchThrown`); the viewer flies it as a projectile of goal 36 (S8b host, `AreaProjectile.Thrower`: free from the start, size arc, the item's own art), the NPC as the attacker row of the crossing; the flight ends at the destination, the range end or the first living critter or object it meets (a stray strike: the item's own hit is not ready), where `LandThrownItem` lays the item on the cell and `FireShotEnd` fires its attacks-a-location event with the thrower as parent (`QueryShotEndItemEvents`), so a grenade's own item spell goes off (GT63). Without a viewer the item lands on the location at once, with no event. **NOT READY:** the dispatcher's shared check against a struck critter (a hit's weight damage, a miss's scatter, TT3) and the line of fire's redirect (the item flies to the location unchecked and no attack event is raised on the struck critter), the explosion of the item's spell for an NPC-held seed item (the cast host's item lookup for a map item lying on the ground; the hero's thrown grenade has the same open lookup, ARC-DEV-259), a real-data smoke leg (no installed NPC holding a grenade is reachable in the smoke maps; the host and the session are tested synthetically), a thrown item that is no grenade (a food, an ammunition, a key), and the thrown weapon's own flight (it still lands at once).

The script goes on whatever happened (GT154). Limits kept honest: the host's gate is the round test only (the can-act test and the reaction slot of 0x4339A0 are not in it, as for the route walk), an NPC's encumbrance is not modelled (the mover reads 0, so the run flag is always set), a tile past the map is the planner's failure, and the viewer without an AI host answers "no AI host" to a walk it cannot start. A negative tile starts nothing (the original's off-map Y of GT158 is not modelled), and the PC and a follower are not walked by a script yet (the hero's click goal and the party drive own them).

### 3.9 Maps, saving, what stays outside

- **Maps.** A run belongs to its owner's map. A map close or reset interrupts every run at level 6 (GS19). The carry flag of GS10 and the destination loader are open (Q1): until it is read the runs of a travelling owner
  end with the map change, as today (the walk, attack, talk, use and cast never cross in the original either; the animations, hit reactions, dying, door runs, eye candy and floating text do).
- **Saving.** MIG13 saves nothing new: a save keeps no run, no goal stack, no projectile and no missile, a load starts with none, as before (MIG14, GQ4 A, saves the table, the spell runs and the objects in flight in
  the next schema). The frame values are plain so MIG14 needs no redesign. If a slice must raise the schema above 70 it adds the upgrade step in `GameSessionSnapshotUpgrade.cs` in the same PR.
- **Presentation clocks** (zoom, panel slide, the art's frame loops that decide nothing, light shimmer if `Light` stays on the viewer) stay on the host as MIG12 §3.7 says. The door's frames are the exception:
  they follow the door's run.
- **Hosts that stay in the viewer** until MIG15: the implementations of the node ports (`IAiGoalWorld` and its descendants), the art and sound choices, the mover's drawing.
- **The turn machine** (`CombatDomain`) and the AP budget stay (G6); K7 (sleep and UI timers as real-time chains) and the spell run's own saved state (MIG14) are not touched.

## 4. Deliberate differences from the original

| Difference | Why |
| --- | --- |
| Typed frame fields instead of the 21 eight-byte parameter slots; the run's work area and the unsaved sound handle are not modelled. | Same behaviour, saveable values; the original's slots are an implementation detail. |
| A run's id is checked by the event as well as its slot (the original compares only the slot and the in-use bit). | A safer match with no visible effect, because the original removes a run's events at every end. |
| Run ids start at 1 and count up (the original seeds its counter randomly, 0..10,000). | Determinism of tests and saves; ids are never shown. |
| No change counter in the handle. | Single player; the counter is 0 there (GS1). |
| An insert at the bottom of a full stack is refused with a typed result. | The original writes a ninth frame past the stack; reachability was not shown (GS14, Q8). |
| A goal with no node yet ends as a typed "unknown goal" instead of running nothing. | Typed failure at the boundary; each slice adds the nodes it owns. |
| Table exhaustion (read again in S2, GT12): the start fails with a typed result, the table sets its flag, and the next step of any run interrupts every run at level 3 (`SweepIfExhausted`). Both rule settings do it; no switch. | The allocator's own log calls it a warning, the flag has one setter and one reader, and the sweep is a designed recovery, so it is a rule of the game, not a bug. 216 slots are never reached by a map's critters in practice. |
| The significant-goal count is derived from the runs (`SignificantGoalCount`), not kept as a counter. | The original counts at pushes and uncounts at the free, each time testing the run's current goal (GT11); the pushes and pops are the stepper's (S4, S5). The two agree unless a run holds counted goals under a "not counted" goal (open question Q3, no case known). |
| A goal with no descriptor (13, 22) cannot start (a typed `NoDescriptor` refusal) and cannot be on a run's stack at any depth (`GoalRun.WithFrames`, `Allocate` and `InsertAtBottom` refuse it), so the interrupt rule always has a descriptor to read for the current goal. | The original reads the descriptor first and has no starter for either goal (GS32; GT39: the pushes of 13 in goals 11 and 12 are dead steps). Q5 (read in S7, GT39) settles it: the nodes that would push 13 are dead (always false), so no door goal ever needs a stacked goal without a descriptor. |
| An owner with no run is not idle (`IsIdle`). | The original addresses memory before the run table there (GT7); not shown to be reachable. |
| A run always holds a goal; the original's empty run (stack top −1) is never kept. | A bottom pop ends the run, and the iterator skips empty runs anyway (GT4). |
| Events fire at their own deadlines inside a frame (GI5), not all against the ping's final clock. | Decision GI5 kept by MIG12; the order of two goal steps in one frame is deadline order, not the host's fixed order. |
| Requests are applied at once and return a typed result; no client request is sent (the network branch of every rule is not followed). | GQ5 A and SA34. |
| The hero's gait and the stick's feel are presentation on top of the goal's steps. | The original draws the same steps; the feel is tuned in the viewer. |
| When the table-full sweep at the stepper's head ends the very run that is about to step (its current goal has priority 3 or less), that run's step is skipped (S5a). Because a map's close leaves the flag (GT22), the first step on the next map sweeps its own run whenever the flag was ever set. | The original goes on stepping a freed slot, which was not shown to be reachable (the 216 slots are never full in practice, and the sweep would have to end the run at hand). |
| An end node is run for each goal's own frame (`AiExecutorRules.EndNode`); the original reads the run's top frame for every goal (GT20). | No difference for the modelled goals: the one end node with a parameter, the cast attempt's spell run (27, 83), belongs to the top goal of its stack. The binding is revisited when a slice adds an end node that reads a lower frame's data. |
| The callback keeps the hero's subturn only while the hero is active and has points (an inactive hero is zeroed by the handler). | The original tests only "a PC-type object with action points left" (GT23, GT24); the active-hero test is the project's rule since the handler existed, so a stunned or knocked-out hero never holds the round with its points. The feed order (before the pending test) is the original's (GT24). |
| The gate for a hero's own run: `FirstStepDelay` compares the round actor's entity (null for the hero) with the run's owner, and the table's start gate compares `RoundActor`. | No hero run is on the table yet; S10 decides the hero's owner string and maps it in both places. |
| The table's start gate covers the six gated starters and the fidget, get-up and wander goals (65, 66); the auto-animation starter (0x435CE0) has no goal known here. | TL41 lists them behind the same gates; the host starts none of them in a round (the executor's `StartGate` is the first line for the fidget after a run). |
| The round's callback is also called by the viewer for the hero, whose actions are no runs yet, and an NPC's event pass re-asks for the event while the hero's viewer-hosted action plays (S5b). | The original's hero actions are slots of the table, whose frees call the callback and whose significant animation would keep the count above 0; until S10–S13 the viewer's idleness stands for it. |
| An NPC whose subturn hook finds no run ends its subturn at once (`RunAiSubturnHook`), instead of setting its points to 0 and calling the callback, which would queue the event and pass the turn 2 ms later (TL39, "a second path does not wait"). | Seen only as the 2 ms and as the stall detector not seeing that NPC; the order of events is the same. Left; S6 did not change the hook. |
| The stalled flag a callback sets is global in the original and is spent on whoever is current when the event falls due, so on the next participant when the subturn changed in the 2 ms between (the original's flag is cleared by the handler and, as TL39 reads, by the round's begin; the session also clears it at a round's end and at the hero's death, where the original's handling was not read). The Original setting keeps that; the Steamwoven setting spends the flag only on the participant the callbacks marked, and drops it when another participant is observed or is current at the handler. | Before ARC-DEV-136 the flag was set and spent in one call, so no setting could show the difference. The switch rides on A11's profile flag (`StallCheckKeepsPreviousActionPoints`), both being the detector's bookkeeping quirks; `AStalledFlagIsSpentOnWhoeverIsCurrentInTheOriginalAndOnlyOnTheMarkedParticipantInSteamwoven`. |
| A pending turn-based event is not cancelled at a round's end or a map's close, and an event pass the viewer skipped for a transfer, reset or reload is asked for again (`RequestTurnBasedEvent`). | The original's removal of a kind-12 event at a round's end was not read; a stale event's pass finds no round, or a participant with points and does nothing, or an NPC without points and passes its turn (GT23). |
| The handler's latent fault (the forced flag set with no current participant dereferences null, GT23) and the callback's reset-flag test (0x402CA0, a global `0x5D10E8` the game-reset path sets, GT24) are not modelled. | The first cannot occur here (a forced flag needs a participant), the second belongs to the game-reset path, which the session does not run through the callback. |
| A free that the original's end step skips (the run's owner handle is null; the run is no longer in use; a top goal of priority 6 or more outside the reset, GT25) is not told apart: every end the table reports calls the callback. | No modelled goal has priority 6 or more at a stepper's end, and an unowned or unused run is not kept in the table. Checked in S6: the critter-state goals have priority 5 or less (dying 5, the others 1 and 3), so none ends at priority 6; revisit with S7–S9 for the door, projectile and eye-candy goals. |
| The critter-state goals start from the session's life step (one choke point, `GameSessionOwner.Life`) for an NPC of the active map, not from the original's damage path, fatigue setter, recovery event and kill; the order inside the original's kill (scripts, round removal, experience) is not reproduced. The first step is 5 animation milliseconds after the start (GS7), where the original's starters write the art synchronously. | One place sees every NPC life change (attacks, spells, poison, fatigue, scripts), so no viewer site can miss one; the visible effects are the same within a frame. |
| The stun starter does not put the critter in combat mode (0x4B3F50). | The original's damage path writes back a stale copy of the critter flags that overwrites that bit (GT37 b), so a critter not in combat mode ends up outside it; the end of the turn (0x4B7C90) stays (the session ends it on incapacitation). |
| The stun end node clears the stunned bit for every end, killed runs included; the original's killed run skips it and leaves the orphaned bit to the AI tick's cleanup (GT35). | Steamwoven has no such tick; a map close or a departure would otherwise leave an NPC stunned for good. |
| "Lying" is derived: unconscious, dead, or a live knock-down run (the knock-down starter's refusal); the host's art per NPC tells the nodes, with a default from the life state for a body no goal has touched. A knock-down of a conscious critter that lies with no run (a get-up refused in a round) is not refused. | The session keeps no art; the case is the rare one GT37 a leaves behind. |
| The stun animation 23 is not read by the viewer (the clip provider answers none), so the stun start refuses on its unreadable header and the stun ends at once, as the former `EndStun` did; the hero's stun is a run of the table since S13a (ARC-DEV-187), ended at its first step for the same reason. | The ART naming of animation 23 is not known; the node machine, the count and the in-round countdown are in place and tested, and a provider that reads the ART makes the stun last. The stun and the get-up poses are drawn only where the ART reads (fall 7, get-up 8, dying 7 and 17–19). |
| The dying goal's end node (0x42FFE0), the pool node's creation (the viewer's, after the run) and node 9's dead flags 0x404 and the notification are not modelled (`DyingFinished` is a port with no action). | The end node's work was not read (S6 read, not read list); the viewer makes the pool when the run is gone, as before. |
| A critter-state start asked for by a life step inside the spell system (spell damage, while the spell-run rules hold the table) waits and runs when the system ends (`DrainCritterStarts`, at most 256 waiting); a stun result that starts no stun run (a knock-down result won, the start was refused) drops the stunned bit at once, as the damage path does. A stunned bit saved with no run behind it (a loaded save) is repaired by the AI gate's cleanup at the next tick, which Steamwoven's gate keeps for a first run that is not the stun (GT35). More than 256 waiting starts are dropped without a count (the viewer then falls back to its former behaviour for the missing run). | The original's starters run synchronously inside the spell's damage; the table's refusal during the spell system is a Steamwoven rule (S3). |
| The anomalies of GT37 are followed (a: the in-round knock-down of a non-current critter ends in a refused get-up and a standing art after 200 ms; d, e, f: turn end and art before a failed start, a stun on a lying critter lost at once); b and g need no code, c (an overlay hides the stun) holds as the rule reads only the first run. | They are in the original's order of calls; none is classified as a bug of the game yet, so no setting was added (open: the D8 classification of GT37 a). |
| (Superseded for the hero's click by S11d, ARC-DEV-176: the click is goal 38 → 11 → 12 and 49 → 50 on the table; the rest of this row still holds for the hero's step through a closed door.) The hero's use of a door and its step through a closed door started the door's own run directly (what node 8 of goal 12 and node 4 of goal 50 do), not through the opener's goals 38 → 11 → 12 and 49 → 50. The viewer decided the open attempt (`AreaPortals.Decide`), charged the 2 action points of the use in a round itself only when the use opens a door (a close and a refusal cost nothing, as GT41 and GT39 read; the hero's step through a door charges nothing where the original charges 2, GT49), plays the locked sound on a refusal, and runs no use script (point 1) or unlock script (point 3: a script-guarded door stays refused); the nodes of the four opener goals are written and tested (`StepDoor`) and wait for the hero's goal run (S10, S11). | The hero is no run on the table until S10 and S11; the door's own run, its pace, sounds, pending close and passage are the original's. In the original a refused attempt ends the use with no sound and no message (GT39, GT51), so the viewer's locked sound and notice are Steamwoven's feedback. |
| (Done by ARC-DEV-209, MIG14 S3: an NPC's walk through a closed door pushes 11 → 12 → 70 as GT49 reads, see [saved runs](saved-runs.md#6-landed-and-not-ready); its planner and step test read the live passages.) An NPC's walk used to pass a closed door the passage rule admits without opening it. | The movers' door step is shared with the hero's walk (S10). |
| (Superseded by ARC-DEV-208, MIG14 S2: the door runs are in the snapshot's `goals` section, [saved runs](saved-runs.md); the rest of this row is the state before it.) The door runs are not saved: a door that is open when the game is saved stays open after the load (no run, so no pending close) until it is used again, and a map's close ends every run at level 6 (an interrupted 70 or 72 leaves the door open for good, an interrupted 71 snaps it shut, GT47). A window move of the main map that no longer holds the door ends its run (the door stays where it stands). | MIG14 saves the carried runs (the doors' goals carry, GS10); until then the load leaves the door as saved. |
| The door's open and close steps write the session's frame one revision per step (the passage rule reads the frame), and the open and close sounds play at the run's begin; the script attachment point 3 (the unlock script) is not fired three or four times per open as the original's nodes do (GT40), and the use node's turn test (0x425840) is not modelled. | One revision a step is the session's way to record a frame; the repeated script calls and the turn test have no visible effect in single player here (the viewer's doors with an unlock script are refused). |
| The anomalies of GT38–GT51 are followed and not classified yet (the D8 "Original"/"Steamwoven" classification is open): the end node of 70 is a net no-op, so an interrupted opening or pending close leaves the door open for good; 71 never looks at the doorway again after 72's test, so a critter that steps in during the last 1.5 s has the door closed on it; the locked sound is unreachable through the goals. | They are the original's order of calls; none looks like a slip of the earlier reading. |

No switch is added by S1 or S2. Where a slice's read of the executable shows a behaviour that is a bug of the game rather than a rule, it is classified by the
[original executable check](../research/original-executable-check.md) and put under the D8 "Original"/"Steamwoven" setting in that slice's PR (candidates still known: the second end-node pass, the animation
events that fall due at once at a round's end, TL38; the table exhaustion was classified a rule in S2).

## 5. Slices

S1 is this document. The codes of S2–S15 are handed out by the coordinator one by one, each checked unused; no code is reserved here. A slice is one PR and updates the coverage rows it changes; the
smoke stages are the ones the change touches (`./arcanum smoke pr --stages …`, one Godot window at a time), the full map chain only in the daily package. The reviews are Opus (rule 2 of the review-model
rule): every slice compares the original, changes the session core or is over 800 lines, except a slice that is only a wording or a row.

The alias and equal-goal tests of the executor's starters still read the NPC's one run (`current`, which is its first run): the host starts no overlay, so reading all runs changes nothing yet and is S5's with the starters' plans. A far goal beyond the planner's reach (Steamwoven corridor, window moves) is handed whole to the route follower (`GoalFrameFlags.RouteFollow`: the goal waits while the route follows it, replans after a retarget); the corridor and the far goals stay the follower's. The tile click no longer sets the route: the table does. **Also:** a route stop (`StopWorldRoute`: an approach, a cast, an attack that replaces the walk) is the level-3 interrupt of the hero's runs too, a retarget keeps a given step of the walk itself, and no step is given over a route that follows a goal of its own. **Not ready:** the location is still the grid actor's commit (S10b-2c), the approach goals (S11) and the held stick (S10c) still drive the route directly, the replan around a companion that the route follower did after a denied step is gone (the original does not re-route a PC), the gait of a step is the viewer's (`StepGait`), not the run flag's. | `HeroWalkGoalsTests` (simulation, 16), `RouteGivenStepTests` (7), `AreaHeroRunsHostTests` (viewer, 24); smoke `walk`, `transfer`, `roundtrip`, `turn-based`

The commit points are gone: `PlaceHeroForDrop` (a drop, an attack, a throw, a projectile's strike) and `PlaceHeroForRound` (combat mode, the round's calls) no longer exist, every reader that used them asks the session now (GR33; the map-mode window moves and map requests still read the grid's cell directly, which is the same cell). **Not ready:** the cell is still committed by the grid actor and written by the viewer, not by the stepper of the table (the hero's mid-step pixel offset and the stick are S10c); a save and a bug report still hand the placement over (it is equal to the session's and changes nothing); a map change stays the change of place's. | Smoke `walk`, `transfer`, `roundtrip`, `turn-based`, `ranged`, `junk`, `ai-turn-based`, `inv-ui`, the Crash Site smoke |
| S10b-2d | ARC-DEV-163, **done** | A follow-up fix of S10b-2b found by the full smoke of ARC-DEV-162 (F2): on `wild` and `ruins` a Ctrl click walked 4 of about 12 cells. **Cause (read from a diagnostic of the failing run):** goal 5's check "the last step must have entered its cell" compared the cell the hero stands on or steps into (`HeroTile`) with the cell of the step it gave one call before. The body begins a given step only at the exact end of the step under way (the follower issues it in the advance that crosses that end), and in a frame the table ticks first and the body advances after (`TerrainViewerApp` runs `TickAreaClock` before `ProcessWorld`), so a call can come while the body is up to a frame behind the table's due time (100 ms in the smokes, 16 ms in the viewer; this frame order is read from the code, the race was not timed against it). In the failing run the call found the body 3 ms short of the end of the step before the queued one: the follower still held the given step, the hero still stood on the cell before it, and the check read a refusal (`StepBlocked`). Why the same smoke did not fail on `main` and `town` is not established (the race depends on where the table's calls fall against the body's step ends, so it is a matter of phase, not of the map). **Landed:** the hero ports have `StepPending(from, to)` (`RouteFollower.GivenStepPending`: the follower still holds the given step and the body has not begun it); goal 5 looks again in a frame (16 ms, as the far goal's poll) when the cell is not reached but the step is pending, and ends the run with `StepBlocked` only when the step is not pending (the grid refused it, or the hero was moved off its cell, the follower's `ROUTE_STEP_DENIED` and `ROUTE_GIVEN_STEP_LOST`). No new read of the executable: the original starts a step on the actor and waits for its end, so a step it began is never "not begun"; the wait is the feet adapter's, not a rule.
