**Order and lanes.** S2 → S3 → S4 → S5a → S5b is one line (each needs the one before). S6–S9 need S5b and are independent of each other in files except the viewer's shared hosts; S10 needs S4 and S5b but not S6–S9; S11 needs S7 and S10; S12 needs S8a–S8c (the cast needs S8a, the throw S8b) and
S11; S13 needs S6 and S12; S14 needs S5b, S6, S9 and S10; S15 is last. `AreaAiHost.cs` is rewritten by S4, S5a, S5b and S6 and also holds the NPC's door use and ranged shots, so S6 runs before S7–S9 and no other lane edits it in that time; S3 shares `FrameStep` and the event registry with MIG14's future
work only. MIG15 (the hosts into the world runtime) runs alongside and closes after MIG14: a slice that leaves a port in the viewer's Core says so in its report. No slice starts before the previous one of its line has merged.

## 6. Risks and open questions

1. **Size of S4 and S5.** (S5 was cut in two, S5a and S5b, by the owner's split rule.) `AreaAiHost` is about 2,250 lines and reads `_runs` from many places (`RunOf`, `Interrupt`, `AnimationRunning`, the casts, the follower catch-up, the round host). S4 moves the storage first
   behind today's calls and changes no behaviour; S5 then makes the rules real. If S4's diff passes about 800 lines of rules the slice is cut again by the owner's split rule.
2. **Frame order.** Today the AI pump runs once per frame after the passes, and the hero's attack, walk and cast run in the viewer's world step in a fixed order. As goal steps are events at their deadlines the order
   of two steps inside one frame becomes deadline order. Smokes that assume the old order (`ai`, `cast`, `ranged`) are restated, not retuned, and the change is listed in S4's report.
3. **The hero's feel.** The grid actor, the stick and the route follower carry tuned input smoothing; the hero as a goal with per-tile steps must not change the walking speed, the gamepad stick or the click-to-walk
   response. S10 keeps the speed rules of `MovementRuntime` and runs the `walk` stage and a manual run of the hero before it merges; the held stick was settled in S10c (the S10c read): the table walks it and the
   grid actor takes no stick intent in an AREA session with a hero host.
4. **`Light` and the round's animation catch-up (TL38).** Settled in S3: the round's second is a jump of game and animation time, a rule (GT18); `Light` stays on the viewer (§3.2).
5. **Projectiles as objects.** S8 needs a transient directory origin for created objects that no save keeps; the snapshot must skip them without a version change and MIG14 replaces the skip. The directory's wipe at a map change
   and a load must remove them. **Settled in S8a (ARC-DEV-139):** `SessionObjectOrigin.Missile` (`missile:n`), kept outside the saved stores, so the snapshot skips it by construction; `CloseGoalMap` wipes the objects with the map and a restored session
   has none. **Settled in S8b (ARC-DEV-140):** `SessionObjectOrigin.Projectile` (`projectile:n`) the same way.
6. **Original questions not yet read.** Q1 (the carried runs' loader), Q2 (the second end-node pass), Q3 (frame flag 0x80), Q6 (spell data goals), Q7 (mode 3 answered by the S10c read, GT105–GT113; the Alt action, the called shot, the use
   node's turn test still open), Q8 ([original goals](original-goals.md#open-questions)). Each is read in the slice that needs it and classified before the rule is written; a question that cannot be settled gets a documented default and a switch.
7. **MIG14 compatibility.** Frames must be savable (entity strings, cells, ints, no objects) and the handles stable; a slice that adds a field a save could not hold says so. The animation events are not saved, so MIG14 must
   re-create them from the saved runs' due times.
8. **Network.** The original's host and client branches of every starter are not followed (GS11, GS17, R6 rows); the request boundary is the place K11 adds them.
9. **Time.** The hero's actions run on animation time now: since MIG12 S7 an open window drops them through the input gate; on the table each window interrupts the hero's run at the original's level (dialog start 3, world map 4, character editor 4, GS20; MIG16 S4 owns the gate). S11 and S12 list each window that assumed a held hero.

## 7. Not ready

S1 landed this document and the update of the K14 line and the MIG13 row. S2 landed the pure rules: `GoalType`, `GoalDescriptors`, `GoalFrame`, `GoalRun`, `GoalRunTable`, `GoalInterruptRules`, `GoalStartRules` and `GoalStarters`
in `Arcanum.Simulation.Goals`, with 61 synthetic tests and 23 rows of the [function registry](../verification/functions.csv). S3 landed the session's one table and the `goal.step` kind on the animation domain, the step
protocol and the frame step's `IGoalStepHost` port, with 27 synthetic tests and one more registry row (0x423C80). S4 put the AI host on the table: the NPCs' runs are session runs stepped by the world
runtime's `GoalStepper` through the viewer's node ports. S5a made the host's interrupts the table's rule: starters with their plans and levels, end nodes for every end, the sweeps at a round's start and at the stepper's head,
the AI pre-check as a query, the object destroy as a kill; no save, schema or setting changed (schema 70). S5b put the round on the table: the callback after every free, the stall detector in it, the
event's handler without its own observation, TL41's 1 ms first frame, the table's start gate and the all-runs duplicate tests; still schema 70 and no setting.

- **Not started.** Everything after S5b. S5b made the round's side of TL39 and TL41 real: the table's frees call the round's "all animations done" callback, which asks for the turn-based event and feeds the
  stall detector, and a goal started for a non-current owner in a round gets TL41's 1 ms first frame. The hero is still a set of viewer hosts, so two bridges stand in for its slots until S10–S13 (an idle hero without
  points calls the callback itself; the event's pass re-asks while the hero's viewer-hosted action plays); an EyeCandy component's eye candy is a run since S9b (spell missiles are runs on created objects since S8a, projectiles since S8b, scenery loops are runs since S9a; the casting goal's own cast animation is still the cast host's), stun and knock-down still cannot interrupt a
  hero's action (B11); doors close by themselves since S7. An NPC whose hook finds no run ends its subturn at once instead of by the callback 2 ms later (§4). The node ports (`IAiGoalWorld`) and the end hooks are still the
  viewer's `AreaAiHost` (MIG15). The executor's equal-goal and alias tests read every run of the NPC since S5b; the host starts overlays since S9b (the EyeCandy components and the hover). The hero's thrown-item return at a failed
  throw runs twice in the original (GT19): S12b decided it: the item stays in the pack until the throw lands, so there is nothing to return twice. Whether the original interrupts or kills an NPC's runs when it is teleported
  to another map is not read: S3 kills them (as 0x423FF0 does for an object removed with its map), since a run belongs to the active map.
- **Not decided here.** (The stick as a held request was settled in S10c, ARC-DEV-147.) (The transient object origin was settled in S8a, risk 5.) The table exhaustion sweep was decided in S2 (a rule, both settings).
- **Not part of MIG13.** Saved runs, spell runs, projectiles and missiles in the snapshot (MIG14); moving the remaining hosts into the world runtime (MIG15); sleep and the UI timers as real-time chains (K7);
  the item, stat, combat-mode and follower-order requests of the boundary; the hero's change-of-place request as a pending request (MIG11's CD7).

## The S2 read of the executable

Behaviour read from the retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52`, `Version.txt` "VERSION:1.0.7.4", the owner's Mac installation; nothing was copied or run) statically with `llvm-objdump` and from the file
image (the name table and the descriptor table), by the reader of ARC-DEV-131 over the rows GS1–GS32 of [the original's goals](original-goals.md), each of which two readers had read: a row of this table that agrees with a GS row is
F2, and the reading adds what the GS rows left open. Addresses are the executable's; behaviour is in prose. The registry rows of the implementing methods cite this section.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Level | Steamwoven (S2) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| GT1 | goal-name table 0x5A164C | 87 pointers to NUL-terminated names, index 0..86, then a NULL; the names are the `ag_` names in the comments of `GoalType`. | F2 (GS8) | `GoalType` (numbers), `GoalTypes.OriginalName` |
| GT2 | descriptor table 0x5B03D0 | 87 pointers then −1; the entries of 13 and 22 are NULL, the other 85 are read entry by entry: priority (+4), no-interrupt (+8), carry (+0xC), "not counted" (+0x10 bit 0), the alias list (+0x14, three entries, −1 ends), the node count (+0, 1..14, read by no code). They are **identical to GS9**: priorities 1 (4 types), 2 (16), 4 (4), 5 (14), 3 (47), the 15 no-interrupt types, the 5 not-counted, the 26 carried, every alias list in the executable's order. | F2 (GS9) | `GoalDescriptors`; the table is a test against GS9 |
| GT3 | "runs goal N" 0x44E830, "equal goal queued" 0x44E710, "first run attacks" 0x44E940 | All three take the **queried** goal's descriptor, walk the candidates (the goal itself, then its alias list, up to three) and compare each with the run's **bottom** goal. The lists are not symmetric (3 lists 7 and 4, 7 lists 4, 3 and 55, 65 lists 7 and 7 does not list 65, 18 lists only 19, 19 lists 18 and 20), so the direction matters: the run is "the same activity" when its bottom goal is in the queried goal's list. 0x44E830 and 0x44E940 look at the first run only; 0x44E710 walks every run and compares the frame's parameter slots 0..5, a mismatch at the sixth not being tested (five object parameters); the starters' duplicate tests call it through the forwarder 0x44E6F0, which passes no out-handle (the caller then learns only whether such a run exists). 0x44E940 also compares the targets through their leaders (0x45DDA0) and has a party-wide branch. | F2 (GS4; the direction was open) | `GoalDescriptors.IsSameActivity(queried, bottom)`; `RunRunningGoal`, `EqualGoalQueued` (target and item), `FirstRunAttacks` (leader port, no party branch) |
| GT4 | run iterator 0x44D2F0 and 0x44D340 | Slot order over the 216 slots; a slot qualifies when its run flag 1 (in use) is set and 2 (done) is clear, its stack top is 0 or more, its slot index is not −1 and its owner equals the object. | F2 (GS4) | `GoalRunTable.RunsOf` |
| GT5 | main run 0x423300, its priority 0x4233D0 | The first qualifying run whose bottom goal's descriptor has no-interrupt 0 is the main run; the priority query answers that descriptor's priority, 0 when there is none. | F2 (GS3, GS4) | `MainRunOf`, `MainRunPriority` |
| GT6 | busy run 0x44E8C0, exact bottom goal 0x44E650 | Busy: the first qualifying run whose bottom goal's carry flag is 0. Exact: the first qualifying run whose bottom goal is N, no alias (a continuation after a given run starts at the next slot without the owner and in-use filter, not modelled). | F2 (GS4, GS10) | `BusyRun`, `RunWithBottomGoal` |
| GT7 | idle test 0x423470 | On the owner's **first** run: stack top 0 (one goal), its current goal 0, 1 or 2, and the animation of the object's art id (field 1 through 0x503E20) is 0. With no run the first-run index is −1, which addresses memory before the table (undefined in the original). | F2 (GS4); the art condition and the no-run case are new | `IsIdle(owner, artAnimationIsIdle)`; no run is not idle |
| GT8 | interrupt rule 0x44E2C0 | The current goal is the frame at the stack top. Order: a run not in use answers 0; during a game reset (0x402CA0) or with an empty stack the run is interrupted with no test; level 6 skips the next two tests; a no-interrupt goal answers 1 and is left; priority 5 answers 0; priority 3 interrupts at level 3 or more, 2 at 2 or more, every other (1 and 4) above its priority. An interrupt marks the run done (0x2); for the slot in the stepping marker 0x5A5978 it answers 1 and stops; otherwise it removes the run's events by slot (0x45BF10), runs the end node of every frame from the top down (none during a reset) and frees the slot (0x44D180 → 0x44CF20). | F2 (GS16, GS18) | `GoalInterruptRules.Verdict`, `GoalRunTable.Interrupt` (the end nodes and events are the caller's) |
| GT9 | per-owner interrupt 0x424070 | The level must be 0..6; a non-zero quiet argument lowers it to 0; every run of the owner in iterator order is interrupted by 0x44E2C0 and the first answer 0 ends the walk with 0 (earlier runs stay ended); an owner with no run answers 1. The network branch comes first and is not followed. | F2 (GS17) | `InterruptOwner(owner, level, quiet)` |
| GT10 | sweeps 0x424250, 0x424290, 0x424310 | 0x424250: while the in-use count is above 0, every slot in use at level 6, a refusal answers 0. 0x424290(level): every slot in use at the level, a refusal only logs. 0x424310: every slot in use whose current goal is not "not counted" (0x44C9A0) at level 3, a refusal logs. | F2 (GS19) | `ClearAll`, `InterruptAll`, `InterruptForRoundStart` |
| GT11 | counting 0x44C840, 0x44C8F0, 0x44C9A0; the free 0x44CF20 | A goal counts (uncounts) when its descriptor has priority 2 or more and no no-interrupt flag and the run's **current** frame is not "not counted" (descriptor +0x10 bit 0 or frame flag 0x80). The free first clears the interface's attack target when the bottom goal is 18 or 19 for the hero, uncounts every frame from the bottom up (each time testing the current frame), clears the slot, decrements the in-use count and, whenever the counter is then 0 and the callback 0x5E34F8 is set, calls it: after **every** free. | F2 (GS22, GS23) | `GoalRun.CurrentGoalNotCounted`, `GoalDescriptors.IsSignificant`, `SignificantGoalCount` (derived, §4), `GoalFreeResult.AnimationsDone` |
| GT12 | allocator 0x44CCB0; stepper head 0x423566 (GS2) | The lowest slot with run flag 1 clear: slot index, run id (the counter, then +1), change counter 0, flags 1 (in use), the owner, current-frame pointer and due time cleared, stack top −1, the in-use count +1. With no free slot it writes a warning to the log that says the animation slots ran out, sets 0x5E34F4 and answers 0 (so the start fails and nothing is queued). The stepper reads 0x5E34F4 first: when set it runs 0x424290(3), clears the flag and then steps its own run. **Classified a rule, not a bug:** the log calls it a warning, the flag has exactly one setter and one reader, and the sweep is a designed recovery; both rule settings follow it, no switch. | F2 (GS1, GS2) | `Allocate`, `ExhaustionPending`, `SweepIfExhausted` |
| GT13 | kill 0x423FF0, 0x44E160 | The per-object kill is skipped while 0x4A7FB0 answers non-zero (the light-scheme hour); else every run of the owner goes to 0x44E160: not in use answers 0; the run gets run flags done and killed (0x8002); the stepped slot answers 1 (the stepper ends it); otherwise events are removed, every frame's end node is called from the top down (they do nothing for a killed run) and the slot is freed; an answer 0 stops the walk. | F2 (GS21) | `Kill`, `KillRun` (the light-scheme gate is the caller's) |
| GT14 | walk starter 0x433640, single-player branch | After the movement gate (0x4339A0), a hero that the always-run rule (0x4304C0) turns into a runner at encumbrance below 4 goes to the run starter 0x433C80. Otherwise 0x44E830(owner, goal 3): with no match it builds the frame without an interrupt, interrupts the owner at level 3 (0x424070, quiet 0) and starts; with a match it compares the **bottom frame's** destination (slot 5, at +0x68 and +0x6C of the run) with the new one: equal answers 1 with no change, different sets the run's path flag 0x4 (+0xC00) and overwrites that destination, answering 1. | F2 (GS14: the third reading) | `GoalStartRules.RetargetWalk`, `GoalRunTable.RetargetDestination` (the bottom frame's `Tile`, the run's `Replan` path flag) |
| GT15 | Q8, not read | Whether the single-player insert beneath a full stack (GS14) is reachable, and how a run whose object no longer resolves is freed when no sweep comes, were **not read** in S2. The first needs no answer for the rule (the insert is refused with a typed result in any case, §4); the second is the stepper's (S4, S5). Q8 stays open in [the original's goals](original-goals.md#open-questions). | not read | `InsertAtBottom` refuses a ninth goal |
| GT16 | GS3 against GS12, GS17, GT8, GT9 | GS3 says a no-interrupt goal "starts at interrupt level 0, which stops nothing", so that it runs beside the main run. Read with the rules, level 0 **ends** nothing, but the verdict is still asked of every run of the owner: a run whose current goal is not an overlay refuses level 0 (its priority is 1 or more and level 0 is not above it), the walk stops there and, at the 27 refusing sites, the start is refused. So an overlay started through 0x44D4E0 (`GoalStarters.Generic`) beside a running main run is refused, and the overlays that must run beside one are the starters that build through 0x44D500 with no interrupt at all (GS3's last sentence; `GoalStarters.NoInterrupt`). S2 codes GS12, GS17 and GT8 as they read; which starter of S7, S9 or S10 uses which plan is that slice's reading of its own call sites. | F2 (GS3 loosely worded, GS12) | `GoalStarters.Generic`, `GoalStarters.NoInterrupt`, `GoalRunTable.InterruptOwner` |

## The S3 read of the executable

Read by ARC-DEV-132 from the same retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52`, the owner's Mac installation) statically with `llvm-objdump`, into the session scratchpad; nothing was copied or run.
Behaviour is in prose; the registry rows cite this section.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Level | Steamwoven (S3) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| GT17 | first frame in 0x44D730, next frame 0x423C80 | The start builds the pair {0, 5} with no conversion and adds the Anim event by delay (0x45B800), so the first step is 5 ms of **animation** time ahead; in a round, for an owner that is not the current participant, it goes straight into the live list through 0x45B820 instead (TL41). The next frame shifts the frame delay left by three (real ms ×8) and, unless the `-animcatchup` flag 0x5DE6D0 is set, adds it from the clock now (0x45B880); with the flag, from the previous due time (0x45BA30). | F2 (GS7, TL40, TL41) | `FirstGoalStepDelayMs` = 5, `GoalStepClockRate` = 8, the repeat from now in `EndGoalStep`; the round's 1 ms path and `-animcatchup` are not followed (S5b; no option) |
| GT18 | round end 0x4B7740, jump 0x45C200 | The round end builds {0, 1,000} (0x45A950) and calls the jump 0x45C200, which adds the pair to the game clock 0x5E8600 and then to the animation clock 0x5E8608 (0x45AA50 each, with carry) and tests nothing; it then sets up the next round (0x4B73A0). The animation list is processed after the real and game lists against its clock as it is then (TL20), so a step due within the second fires once in the same ping when the round ends inside the turn-based handler. TL38's inference is confirmed. A rule of the game: every jump moves the two clocks together (TL26). | F2 (TL26, TL38) | The session's round end is the same jump (`ClockAdvanceReason.ExplicitWait` moves game and animation); a pending step due inside it fires at the next pass; both settings; no switch |

## The S5 read of the executable

Read by the readers of ARC-DEV-135 (one reader per row, Opus agents; no second reader yet) from the same retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52`, the owner's Mac installation) statically with `llvm-objdump`, into the session scratchpad;
nothing was copied or run. Behaviour is in prose. GT19 and GT20 answer open question Q2 of [the original's goals](original-goals.md#open-questions); GT21 and GT22 settle two points of the slice's own review.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Level | Steamwoven (S5a) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| GT19 | the end nodes 0x42B090, 0x429CD0, 0x42FFE0, 0x431B20, 0x42DED0, 0x430F20, 0x42CAC0, 0x42B440, 0x42C650, 0x42AFB0, 0x42B250, 0x42B640, 0x42B6F0, 0x429E70, called by the end step's loop (0x4239FF–0x423A72) and the interrupt's loop (0x44E45D–0x44E49F) | Both loops call each goal's end node (descriptor entry +0x1E0; 59 goal types have none) over the stack from the top down, after the parameter set-up 0x44DD80; between the two passes the stepper changes only the run's path bit, the done mark and the stepping marker, and the owner-run walk skips a done run, so an interrupt made from inside an end node never re-enters the dying run. In single player, after their guards (a map being cleared, the killed bit), the nodes reset the object's art to animation 0 and frame 0 (0x42B090 for 22 goal types, also inside 0x42B440, 0x42B250, 0x42AFB0, 0x429CD0), end a spell run once (0x429CD0, goals 27, 74 and 83: it writes "none" over the run's slot in the top frame and every lower frame with the same id, so a second call releases nothing), clear overlay, light and sound slots (0x429CD0, 0x431B20, 0x42DED0: set to none), destroy the owner, the dead body or the projectile behind an object flag the second call finds set (0x429E70, 0x42FFE0, 0x42CAC0), clear the stunned bit (0x42B250), toggle a door to the same end state (0x42B640, 0x42B6F0) or unconceal (0x42AFB0, whose repeat only restarts a fidget in the same event). **All are idempotent but one: 0x42B440, the throw's end node (goal 37).** For a **player character** with an item in the frame's slot 3 it returns the item (a free inventory cell through 0x466640, else the tile through 0x466E50) and never clears the slot; the throw node clears it only at a successful throw, so after a failed throw (before the action frame, nodes 2 to 7 into the end step) the item is returned in the end step's pass and again in the interrupt's pass: a second list entry and count, or a second tile placement (the window and weight consequences, the stack-merge branch and a double tile placement were not read). An NPC's throw skips the return (a type check at 0x42B522), so the NPC loses the item once, as TA1 says. | F1 (one reader, medium-high; the repeat of the unconceal node medium) | The host runs the end nodes of every end **once** (`EndNodes`, `StepHooks`); for an NPC the original's second pass is invisible for the 26 modelled goals, so nothing is added. The hero's throw is no run yet: it is the hero's throw run (S12) that decides the exception, and it is classified there (a bug of the game: the item is returned twice) with the D8 "Original"/"Steamwoven" switch (Original repeats the return, Steamwoven returns once), after a second reader has repeated this one. |
| GT20 | parameter set-up 0x44DD80 for an end node | An end node reads the parameters of the run's **current** (top) frame, not of the goal it is called for: the set-up reads the frame at the run's top pointer (set at 0x42368D, or 0x44E427 when null), which neither loop moves, and 0x429CD0, 0x431B20, 0x42DED0, 0x42CAC0, 0x42B440 and 0x42C650 also read or write that frame directly. Where a pushed goal copied its parent's data (EX5) the two agree; they differ only where a pushed goal overwrote a slot such as 3, 11, 14–16 or 20 (medium). Not read: whether the owner's destroy inside the first pass also sets the killed bit of a done run (0x423FF0 against 0x44E160), and the side effects of 0x4F0950 and 0x4F0A90 beyond the art frame. | F1 (one reader, high for the binding) | `AiExecutorRules.EndNode` passes each goal's own frame (a deliberate difference, §4): the only modelled end node with a parameter, the cast attempt's spell run (27, 83), belongs to the top goal of its stack, so both bindings agree. |
| GT21 | the AI follower catch-up 0x4AD4D0, the waypoint move 0x4ACBB0 and the standpoint move 0x4ACDB0; the interrupt at 0x4AD591, 0x4ACD17, 0x4ACFC9; a talk check at 0x4AD903 | Each of the three calls the per-object interrupt 0x424070 at level 4 and **drops its answer**: the catch-up goes straight on to the leader's position and the move 0x43E770 (0x4AD5AD), the waypoint and standpoint moves go on to 0x43E770 on their equal-values branch (0x4ACD24, 0x4ACFDE) and return 1. So an NPC whose current goal refuses level 4 (a spell attempt, priority 4) is still moved while its run goes on. Another level-4 call at 0x4AD37F also ignores its answer; the level-2 call at 0x4AD903 (the talk check) tests it and answers "busy" on a refusal. | F1 (one reader, high) | `InterruptAnimations`, `MoveToLeader`, `Relocate` and `RelocateFollower` interrupt at level 4 and move whatever the interrupt answers (`A_standpoint_move_interrupts_at_level_4_and_moves_whatever_the_interrupt_answers`). |
| GT22 | the table-full flag 0x5E34F4: the allocator 0x44CCB0 (0x44CD09), the stepper head 0x423560 (0x423566, 0x42357F, 0x423587) | Exactly three absolute references: the allocator sets the flag when no slot is free, the stepper's head reads it and, when set, runs the level-3 sweep 0x424290 and clears it. No map reset 0x40EBA0, map clear 0x45BCE0, sweep 0x424250, game reset path or module entry touches it, so a map change leaves a set flag standing until the next step of any run, which then sweeps (a bulk clear through a runtime pointer was not excluded statically, nothing points to one). | F1 (one reader, high) | `GameSessionOwner.CloseGoalMap` keeps the flag; the next step on the new map sweeps once (`A_map_close_keeps_the_table_full_flag_so_the_next_maps_first_step_sweeps_once`). |

## The S5b read of the executable

Read by one Opus reader in ARC-DEV-136 (no second reader yet) from the same retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52`, the owner's Mac installation) statically with `llvm-objdump`, into the session scratchpad;
nothing was copied or run. Behaviour is in prose. GT23 and GT24 settle two points the S5b design had left as inference (the wait for the NPC's own run, the order of the pending test and the stall feed); GT25 fixes the order of a stepper's free and the subturn hook.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Level | Steamwoven (S5b) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| GT23 | the turn-based event handler 0x4B7150 (kind 12) | The handler tests nothing about animations: it never reads the significant-goal counter 0x5E3504, never calls 0x423300 or the run iterators 0x44D2F0 and 0x44D340, and checks no interface or PC animation and no other busy flag. In order: it calls the trace helper 0x4B6DA0 (result ignored); with the round flag 0x5FC22C at 0 it returns 1 and does nothing; if the current participant 0x5FC240 is set, is a PC (0x40DA20: object field 0x14F equal to 15) and its action points 0x5FC234 are above 0, it returns; if the forced flag 0x5FC264 is set it clears it and pays the remaining points with 0x4B7CD0; if the points (0x4B7780) are still above 0 it returns; otherwise it calls the next subturn 0x4B7690. It returns 1 on every path. Latent fault: with the forced flag set and no current participant the payment dereferences null. | F1 (one reader, high) | `GameSessionOwner.CompleteCombatActions` (partial; the same order, the hero's keep-turn test also requiring the hero to be active, §4) observes nothing; `AreaTurnBased.Step` runs it for an NPC whatever the NPC's own run plays (`The_event_pass_passes_the_turn_of_an_npc_without_points_whatever_its_own_run_is_playing`, `A_stalled_npc_with_a_run_that_began_before_the_event_has_its_rest_spent_by_the_event_pass`). |
| GT24 | the animation callback 0x4B7080 | In order: the round flag 0x5FC22C at 0 returns (0x4B708B); the trace helper is called; the reset flag (0x402CA0, global 0x5D10E8) non-zero returns (0x4B70A9); only when the current participant is set, a PC with action points above 0 returns (0x4B70D3), and this test does not look at whether the PC is active; then the stall feed (0x4B70D5–0x4B7111); only then the pending test (0x45C0E0 with kind 12 at 0x4B7119: an event already pending returns) and, when none is, the kind-12 event two milliseconds ahead (0x45A950, 0x45B800). So the detector is fed even when an event is pending, and is not fed for a PC with points left or with no current participant. The feed: the same handle as the stored pair 0x5FC258/0x5FC25C sets the forced flag when the points equal the stored points 0x5FC260 and in both cases stores the current points; a different handle stores only the handle and leaves 0x5FC260 at the previous participant's points (A11). | F1 (one reader, high) | `GameSessionOwner.AnimationsDone` feeds the detector before the schedule, whether or not an event is pending (`CombatRoundTests`: the stall and pending tests); the reset-flag test is not modelled and the hero's active test is the project's rule (§4). |
| GT25 | the stepper's end of a done run 0x423560 (free at 0x423B50, hook at 0x423B8D), the run free 0x44E2C0 with level 6, 0x44D180, 0x44CF20 | After a run's step ends it, the stepper sets the "run being stepped" id 0x5A5978 to −1 and calls 0x44E2C0 (run, 6) at 0x423B50; with the id at −1 it frees the run: removes its kind-1 events (0x45BF10), runs each level's end node (0x44E407), calls 0x44D180 (0x44E4A7), which always reaches the slot free 0x44CF20 synchronously (directly at 0x44D22B or through 0x44D0E0 at 0x44D16E). The slot free decrements the significant-goal counter for each level (0x44C8F0 at 0x44D02E), clears the slot and, at 0x44D09D–0x44D0B3, calls the callback 0x4B7080 when the counter is 0 and the callback pointer 0x5E34F8 (set by 0x423FE0, called by the round start at 0x4B722C) is set. Only after the free returns does the stepper read the round flag (0x4B6D70) and, for an owner of type 15 or 16, call the subturn hook 0x4B7010 (0x423B8D). So the order is free, callback, hook. Exceptions: a run whose owner handle (+0x20/+0x24) is null skips both free and hook (0x423B4B); 0x44E2C0 with level 6 returns 0 without freeing when the run is no longer in use (bit 0 of +0xC clear, 0x44E338), or, outside the reset flag, when the top goal's priority (+4) is 6 or more and not 2 or 3 (0x44E3DF), and the hook still follows. | F1 (one reader, high; the exceptions read, not traced further) | The table reports each ended run and the session calls the callback for it in the same call (`FreedRuns`), before the host's next frame plays the hook (the placement of the hook stays the round host's, §3.7); the exceptions are not modelled (§4). |

## The S6 read of the executable

Read by one Opus reader in ARC-DEV-137 (no second reader yet) from the same retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52`, the owner's Mac installation) statically with `llvm-objdump` and from the file image (descriptor table 0x5B03D0), into the session scratchpad;
nothing was copied or run. Behaviour is in prose. GT26 to GT37 answer what the earlier rows left open for the stun, knock-down, get-up and dying goals (GS9, GS13, GS31, DA1–DA6); a node table is written "node: function (false step / true step)", "restart" is the step 0x10000000 (node 1 again), "end" 0x90000000, "pop" 0x30000000, "push N" 0x40000000 + N, and −2 is the run's stored frame delay.
Not read: the clock of the kind-8 recovery event; 0x430490, 0x4D5620, 0x459740, 0x457450, the NPC branch of the kill after the experience award, 0x504100, 0x460520; the dying goal's end node 0x42FFE0; who gets a lying conscious critter up in a round when it is not the current participant; the meaning of critter flag 0x40 and object flag 0x10000 beyond their tests (taken as paralysis and stone from DA5).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Level |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| GT26 | goal 57 `ag_animate_stunned`, descriptor 0x5AC7D0 (priority 1, carried, no aliases, 7 nodes, end node 0x42B250) | Node table: 1 0x42CA90 animation-playing test (false: 2 / true: 3); 2 0x42E070 stun frame step (7 / restart after −2); 3 0x426F10 lies in its fall (4 / **push 63 get-up**); 4 0x426E80 NPC shown in the concealed animation 5 (5 / **push 54 unconceal**); 5 0x42DF40 stun start (6 / restart); 6 0x433270 (end / end); 7 0x426F60 countdown (5 / end). So a stunned critter that lies fallen first gets up, a concealed NPC first unconceals, then animation **23** plays from frame 0, one frame per stored delay, and each time it reaches its last frame the countdown runs: while the count stays above 0 the animation starts again at frame 0 at once; at 0 or below the goal ends. The stun start sets the art to animation 23 frame 0 **before** its guards, then refuses (false, so node 6 and the end) for an object with object-flag bit 0x10000 (a stoned object, as DA5 names it) or a critter with critter-flag 0x40; otherwise it sets the art again, stores the delay 1000 / the ART's frame rate and the playing flag and answers true. When the ART header does not read it logs, stores 100 ms and answers **false** (the stun ends at once; unlike the knock-down and dying starts, DA3, DA5, which go on). The frame step refuses (false, with the playing flag still set) for the stoned bit and for a critter with flag 0x40 outside animations 17..19; at the last frame it clears the playing flag and answers false (the frame stays); one frame before the action frame it sets run flag 0x4; otherwise it steps one frame (0x43F7F0) and answers true. | F1 |
| GT27 | the stun countdown node 0x426F60, the turn-based countdown 0x427000 (called only from the participant test 0x4B7580), the stun end node 0x42B250 | The count lives in the frame's slot 18 (+0x98). **Out of a round** the node decrements it by one per full play of animation 23 and, at 0 or below, clears the stunned bit and answers true (end). **In a round** (0x5FC22C set) the node does not decrement: the animation loops. Instead, whenever the round loop (round set-up 0x4B7470, next subturn 0x4B76CD/0x4B76FC) reaches a participant that **cannot act** (0x45D790 false), 0x4B7580 skips that participant's turn and calls 0x427000, which, for the current participant with the stunned bit whose **first** run is goal 57 (0x44E830), takes **3** from the count and, at 0 or below, interrupts that run at level 6 (the end node then clears the bit). The turn on which the count runs out is still skipped. In multiplayer the client branch never decrements (not followed). The end node 0x42B250 (skipped for a killed run, run flag 0x8000, and while 0x410060 answers non-zero): for a PC or NPC sets the art to animation 5 when the critter is concealed (0x45EDE0) else 0, frame 0, calls 0x430490 (not followed), and clears the stunned bit; it runs on every end, interrupt or pop of the goal. | F1 |
| GT28 | stun starter 0x4357B0 (single caller: the damage path 0x4B4962) | Parameter: the critter only. Gate 0x45D790 "can act": not destroyed or off (field 0x13 bits 0x3), alive (0x45D8D0: hit points above 0), conscious (0x45D800: an undead, critter flag 0x4, or fatigue above 0), critter flags 0x20 and 0x40 both clear, object-flag 0x10000 clear. So **a stunned (or 0x40) critter cannot be stunned again**: that is the duplicate rule. Then the level-4 interrupt (GS13) and the build of goal 57 through 0x44D4E0 (level 1); then, **before the start**: 0x4B3F50 puts the critter in combat mode when it is not (0x4B3D50, then the toggle 0x4B3F80), 0x4B7C90 ends its turn when it is the current participant of a round (action points to 0 and the next subturn 0x4B7690), and the count is set to (20 − constitution) / 2, truncated toward zero (stat 2 through 0x4B0490); then the start. A refusal at the gate, either interrupt or the build answers 0 with nothing written; the turn end and the combat mode stay even if the final start fails. The starter never sets the stunned bit: the damage path does (GT31). Duration: the animation plays max(count, 1) times out of a round (constitution 20 or more still plays once); in a round the critter loses ceil(max(count,1)/3) turns, the first being the one the stun ended (if current). | F1 (GS13's levels and the turn end confirmed) |
| GT29 | goal 62 knock-down, descriptor 0x5AD1D0 (priority 3, carried, 4 nodes, **no end node**); starter 0x435A90 (callers: damage 0x4B49E0, fatigue setter 0x45D64C, the slip in the walk step 0x430DF4, the spell component 0x45160B) | Node table: 1 0x42CA90 (2 / 3); 2 0x42CC80 animate step (4 / restart after −2); 3 0x42E4B0 fall start (end / restart); 4 0x42E460 (pop / **replace by 63 get-up after 200**). The fall start sets the art to animation **7** frame 0, writes it to slot 8, stores 1000 / frame rate (100 ms with a log when the header does not read, still true) and the playing flag (DA5, confirmed; no sound). The animate step is BP5's (DA5's extra guards confirmed: stoned → false; a critter with flags 0x60 only in animations 7, 11, 17..19; animation 14 with flag 0x4 of field 0xDB holds without stepping). After the last frame node 4 asks 0x45D800: an **unconscious** critter pops the goal, so the run ends and the critter keeps animation 7 at its last frame (no end node resets it); a conscious one is replaced by get-up 63 after a 200 delay (AG1 milliseconds, animation clock, GT17). Starter: refuses a null object, a critter whose art already shows animation 7 at any frame (**the duplicate rule: no knock-down of a lying critter**) and a dead one (0x45D8D0); level-5 interrupt (not quiet), build through 0x44D4E0 (level 3), start. It neither ends the turn nor writes a flag. | F1 (DA5 and GS13 confirmed) |
| GT30 | goal 63 get-up, descriptor 0x5AD3D0 (priority 3, **not carried**, 4 nodes, end node 0x42B090); starter 0x434DE0 (callers: recovery event 0x45E75E, heal 0x4B5DEC, a script action at 0x44916E; also pushed by goal 57 node 3, by goal 62 node 4, and by talk, GS29) | Node table: 1 0x42CA90 (2 / 3); 2 0x42CC80 (pop / restart after −2); 3 0x42E590 get-up start (4 / restart); 4 0x433270 (end / end). The get-up start refuses (false) a critter that cannot act (0x45D790) and, **in a round, any critter that is not the current participant**; for the current participant it charges **5 action points** (0x4B7CD0; a failed payment ends the turn and refuses). Otherwise it sets animation **8** frame 0 (slot 8), the delay (100 ms with a log and an assertion when the header does not read), the playing flag, true. At the last frame the goal pops; the end node 0x42B090 then sets animation 0 (5 when concealed) frame 0 for a PC or NPC. A refused start goes to node 4 and the end step, so the **end node still sets the standing art at once** (GT34). The starter: gate 0x4348E0 with cost 0 (can act; out of a round always; in a round only the current participant, an NPC with points below the cost passing only with fatigue above 35 % of its maximum, a PC always), art showing animation 7 at any frame, build through 0x44D4E0 (level 3, no fixed interrupt), slot 8 = the art with animation 8, start; no turn end. | F1 |
| GT31 | damage path 0x4B4390 (0x4B4906–0x4B4A66), fatigue setter 0x45D5A0, fatigue recovery event 0x45E610 (kind 8, mode 0), heal 0x4B58C0, the node 0x433270 | **Stun**: result flag 0x40, the critter not already stunned and neither 0x80 nor 0x100 in the result: a combat message (0x4CBB80), the stunned bit is added to a copy of the critter flags read **before** the starter, the starter runs, a failed start clears the bit from the copy, and the copy is written back (0x406D40), then a log entry (0x10). **Knock-down**: either of 0x80 or 0x100: messages 0x4CBBF0, 0x4CBE00, then the knock-down starter; with 0x100 (knock-out), unless already unconscious, fatigue damage is raised by the critter's remaining fatigue plus the combat's fatigue damage (+0x64; 10..20 random in multiplayer) through the fatigue setter, log entry 0x11, else 0x12. So a knock-down result wins over a stun result. The **fatigue setter** writes field 0xE2 (clamped at 0), and with damage left schedules the kind-8 recovery event (mode 0, 80,000 of its clock, no duplicate: 0x45C140 with the matcher 0x45E8D0); a conscious critter that becomes unconscious gets 0x457450 (not followed), a message, the knock-down starter and the turn end 0x4B7C90. The **recovery event** (mode 0) skips a dead critter, lowers the fatigue damage by stat 12 times the steps (FatigueRecoverySteps), and when the critter was at fatigue 0 or below and is now above, **starts get-up at once** (no delay). **Heal** (fatigue amount +0x54) and the script action do the same: get-up when the heal lifts fatigue from 0 or below to above 0. Node 0x433270 (the last node of 57 and 63, and of 31 when its pool node has no object): for an object that is not the hero, pays 2 action points of the current participant through 0x4B7CD0 (in a round only; a failed payment ends the turn); its answer does not matter, both steps end the run. | F1 (GS31's 0x180 reading confirmed and split) |
| GT32 | become prone 0x435B30 (single caller: the script action switch at 0x449810, script action 93 per GS20) | No goal: level-4 interrupt (refusal answers 0), then the art is set to animation 7 at its **last** frame (frame 0 with a log when the header does not read); answers 1. No flag, no turn end, no get-up is scheduled: the critter lies until something starts get-up or resets its art. | F1 (GS13 confirmed) |
| GT33 | goal 31 `ag_dying`, descriptor 0x5A93D0 (priority 5, carried, 9 nodes, end node 0x42FFE0); starter 0x435080 (single caller: the kill 0x45DA20 at 0x45DC43) | Node table: 1 0x42CA90 (2 / 3); 2 0x42D6F0 (7 / restart after −2); 3 0x426F10 lies in its fall (4 / **7**); 4 0x426E80 (5 / 5); 5 0x42BC10 (7 / 6); 6 0x42D570 (7 / restart); 7 0x42FED0 pool (8 / 9); 8 0x433270 (end / end); 9 0x42FF40 (end / end). DA2–DA6 confirmed: node 5 reads the kill's animation from slot 14 (+0x78, which the starter writes from its third argument), sets the art and writes it to slot 8 (+0x48), which node 6's dying start reads (descriptor data 8); node 5 also takes the critter out of combat mode (0x4B3F20). A critter already lying in its fall (animation 7 at any frame) skips the dying animation and goes straight to the pool. The pool node creates the pool (0x432D90: proto 4028 at the critter's location, goal 0 on it through 0x44D4E0, the one-day decay event 0x45EBE0; a failure destroys it) unless the critter's tile blocks (0x4D7110), and always answers true, so node 8 runs only for a missing object. **Node 9**, unless run flag 0x200 is set, for a critter with hit points 0 or below sets object flags 0x404 (0x43D0E0), in single player calls 0x460520 when the critter is the hero (the death screen, not followed), and calls 0x4CBC60 with no killer; then the run ends. So the delay before the pool is the dying animation itself (frames − 1 stored delays), with no other wait node. Starter: no gate besides a null object; build through 0x44D4E0 at priority 5 (ends priorities 1–4, refused by a priority-5 run such as knockback 43), slot 14 = the animation, start; its answer is ignored by the kill. | F1 (DA1–DA6 confirmed node by node) |
| GT34 | the kill 0x45DA20, in order | (1) 0x4D5620 (an object-flag 0x200000 list, not followed); (2) the script attachment point 12 on the victim with the killer (0x441980); **a zero answer cancels the death**; (3) a destroyed victim (field 0x13 bit 0x1) stops here; (4) out of combat mode (0x4B3F20); (5) field 0xF4 = the time now (0x45AAE0); (6) 0x459740 (not followed); (7) in a round, 0x4B80E0 rewrites bits 14–20 of field 0xDB and **removes the dead critter from the participant list** 0x5FC1F8 (0x4CD940); (8) for an NPC: 0x45ED70, field 0x11B = the killer, the AI's kill notice 0x4AA1B0, the killer's PC (or the killer's leader 0x45DDA0) gets the experience (0x45F110, a fifth), field 0x11D cleared, 0x45DC90, 0x4F5330, the script attachment point 20 for every object of the list 0x4411C0 builds, then 0x45DE00/0x45DFC0/0x45DE50 and 0x4BAB30; for a PC 0x467520 (these were not followed); (9) for a critter art, a reset of the art through 0x504100 (not followed); (10) **the dying goal starts**; (11) unless field 0xDB bit 0x40, the one-day kind-24 event 0x45EBE0 on the body; (12) hit-point damage = 32,000 (0x43D530, field 0x1D); (13) 0x4CBC60 with the killer. So the death scripts and the round removal come **before** the dying run; the hit points are written after it starts; the flat/no-block flags (0x404) and the hero's death screen come at the **end** of the dying run (node 9), not at the kill. | F1 (step 2's veto and step 7 high; steps marked "not followed" were not read) |
| GT35 | the AI tick's pre-check 0x4A8570 | In order: a dead critter, one with field 0x13 bits 0x102, a destroyed leader, a stoned critter, a critter whose 0x4C1110 answer is set (after an optional callback); then the script attachment points 16 (in combat mode, out of a round) and 15 fire; then, in a round, a critter that is not the current participant stops, and so does **a main run whose bottom goal's priority is above 2** (0x4233D0) all stop the tick. So knock-down 62, get-up 63 (priority 3) and dying 31 (5) stop it, and **stun 57 (1) passes this test**; but the next tests stop it anyway: after the random cures of critter flags 0x80 (1 in 160) and 0x700 (1 in 8000), **a critter with the stunned bit** returns at once, and when its first run is not goal 57 (0x44E830) the tick **clears the stunned bit** first (an orphaned stun bit heals at the next AI tick); then an unconscious critter returns. A stun, a knock-down, a get-up and dying therefore all keep the AI from starting anything; the AI does not interrupt them. | F1 |
| GT36 | interactions (from GT26–GT35 with GS12, GS13, GT8) | Stun and knock-down sit on the critter's main run: their starters interrupt every run of the critter (4 and 5), so they end walks, attacks and priority-3 goals (and get-up 63 and knock-down 62 end each other as priority-3 goals; a knock-down also ends a stun, whose end node clears the stunned bit and stands the art). A stun is refused while a spell attempt (priority 4) or any priority-5 run is current, and then the damage path drops the stunned bit; a knock-down is refused only by priority 5. Overlays are untouched. No second stun while the stunned bit is set; no second knock-down while animation 7 shows (a second one in the first 5 ms before node 3 replaces the first, as the knock-out path does: damage then the fatigue setter both call the starter). A stun on a critter lying in a conscious knock-down pushes get-up, whose start refuses because the critter is now stunned; node 4's end step then ends the run: both end nodes run, the art stands at animation 0 and the stunned bit is cleared, so the stun is lost (GT37). Dying ends everything below 5 and cannot be interrupted below level 6; stun and knock-down refuse a dead critter. | F1 (inference from the rows above where they combine; the end step ending the whole run follows AG1) |
| GT37 | anomalies, not classified | (a) **In a round a knock-down of a critter that is not the current participant** (the usual case: it is hit on its attacker's turn) ends in goal 63's refused start: node 4, end step, end node 0x42B090, so the critter shows animation 0 at once, 200 ms after its fall, with no get-up animation and no action points paid. Likewise any refused get-up (cannot act, unpaid points) leaves the art standing. Looks like an oversight; not observed in play. (b) The damage path writes back a copy of the critter flags read **before** the stun starter, whose combat-mode entry (0x4B3F80 sets 0x40000) is thereby overwritten when the critter was not in combat mode. (c) A stunned critter's in-round countdown (0x427000) and the AI tick's stunned-bit cleanup look only at the **first** run (0x44E830, GS4): an overlay in a lower slot hides the stun, so in a round the count never falls (the stun then lasts until another interrupt) and out of a round the AI tick drops the stunned bit while the stun animation goes on (inference). (d) The stun starter ends the turn and enters combat mode before the start, which may still fail (table full). (e) A stun on a lying conscious critter is lost at once (GT36). (f) The stun start changes the art to animation 23 before refusing a stoned or 0x40 critter; the end node then resets it. (g) The kill ignores a refused dying start (GS12's inference confirmed: no dying run under a priority-5 knockback). | F1 (a high for the path, the gameplay effect not observed; b high; c inference; d–g high) |

## The S7 read of the executable

Read by one Opus reader in ARC-DEV-138 (no second reader yet) from the same retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52`, the owner's Mac installation) statically with `llvm-objdump` and from the file image (descriptor table 0x5B03D0, goal names 0x5A164C); nothing was copied or run. Behaviour is in prose. GT38 to GT51 answer what GS8, GS29, GS31 and Q5 left open for the door goals; the notation is that of [the S6 read](#the-s6-read-of-the-executable). The door goals keep the door in frame slot 3 and the opener in slot 0; the door's own runs (70, 71, 72) keep the door in slot 0 and the opener in slot 4. Every delay goes through the next-frame function 0x423C80, so 1500 is 1.5 s in the clock of GT17 (the animation clock's ×8 shift), and the stored frame delay is 1000 / the ART's rate.
Not read: how the walk step 0x42E9B0 / 0x427110 / 0x425760 treats the door in the 5 ms before 70's first step; what the stepper does with a door run whose sector is not loaded (0x44DD80's locator refresh); spells that lock, unlock or open; the call at 0x4A1688 in 0x49D690 (probably network); any interface "locked" message; the argument order of 0x441980 beyond "door, opener, script point".

Node counts: 11 five, 12 eight, 49 two, 50 four, 70 four, 71 three, 72 one. The no-interrupt flag is set only on 12 and 50. Carried: 11, 12, 49, 70, 71 and 72 (50 is not). End nodes: 70 has 0x42B640, 71 has 0x42B6F0 (door in slot 0); 11, 12, 49, 50 and 72 have none. Goal 13 has no descriptor and is unreachable (GT39). | F2 (table read twice, GS8 agrees) |

Nodes 4 and 5 are dead: 4 repeats the attempt and is always false (5 / push 13), 5 always answers 0 (end / push 15). So a locked, jammed, held or script-refused door **ends the opener's whole run** (a walk or use that pushed 11 included) with no sound and no message and never reaches goal 13; the attempt runs twice here, so the unlock script (point 3) fires twice. | F1 |

Closing costs nothing extra: no action points, script, lock or jam test, no capability test; only a magically held door refuses. A door that is opening (70) or pending (72) counts as not idle, so the close interrupts it. | F1 |

The begin makes the **first open step** at once (the door jumps to the first frame of its range and the object flags 0x430 are set, so it is passable from the start; a refused step — a non-portal, an ART lookup failure, a one-frame ART, the last frame already — answers false), stores the delay at +0xCFC (1000 / the ART's rate, unsigned; 100 ms with a log when the header does not read), clears run flags 0x4 and 0x8, plays the **open sound** (portal sound kind 0 on the door), sets 0x10 and answers true. Open or closed is only the ART frame (0 = closed) plus the flags 0x430; no portal-flag bit changes. The end node 0x42B640 is skipped for a killed run (0x8000), while 0x410060 answers non-zero or for a non-portal; an open door (any frame but 0) is left alone, a closed one that is not magically held is toggled open and closed again (**a net no-op**, no sound). | F2 (begin, step, delay and sound agree with LK11; end node F1) |

Dead bodies, items and critters on the tile across the edge do not block, and the opener is not tested specially: it blocks only when it stands on the door's tile. The test repeats every 1500 and **after a clear test the closing still waits 1500** before 71 starts; nothing looks at the doorway again in that wait. | F1 |
| GT45 | goal 71 `ag_animate_door_closed`; nodes 0x42CA90, 0x42D2A0, 0x42D1C0; end node 0x42B6F0; step 0x4F0A90 | 1 (2 / 3). 2: 0x42D2A0 one close step (**pop after −2** / restart after −2). 3: 0x42D1C0 begin (pop / restart after −2): the first close step (from the last frame, one frame down), the delay stored as in 70, the **close sound** (kind 1), flag 0x10, true. Each later step is one frame per delay; the step that leaves the first frame of the range sets frame 0 and **clears 0x430**, so the door blocks only at the **end** of the animation; the next step refuses at frame 0 and the run ends one more delay later. The end node 0x42B6F0 has the guards of 70's: a door still open and not magically held is toggled closed at once (no sound), a closed one is left. | F2 (agrees with LK10, LK11; end node F1) |
| GT46 | the timing (GT42–GT45, GT17, TL41) | Opening a three-frame range with the delay d = 1000 / the ART's rate: the run starts 5 ms after the start (1 ms in the live list in a round: the door is never the current participant); frames at 0, d and 2d; 72's first test at 3d + 1500; 71's first close step at 3d + 3000; closed after three more delays, the run ending one delay later. Every wait is the stored −2 delay or the literal 1500, all in the clock of GT17. A walker's push of 11 carries a 50 ms delay (GT49). | F1 (the arithmetic is inference from the tables) |
| GT47 | interrupts of door runs | An interrupted 70 leaves the frame and flags where they are (partly or fully open), with **no close pending**. An interrupted 72 has no end node, so the door stays open for good. An interrupted 71 **snaps the door closed** (unless magically held). Who interrupts: a new 0x4246E0 start (any open or close), the full-table sweep of GS2 (level 3), the level-6 interrupts (a map's close); a game reset runs no end nodes. | F1 (rows) / inference (who) |
| GT48 | rounds (0x42B7F0, 0x4246E0, 0x4B7CD0, TL41) | In a round **no door closes**: 72 fails on the global round flag alone and retries every 1500; it reads neither the opener nor the current participant, so TL41's "for a non-current opener" reads "any round". The door's runs pass through no gate and 70 and 71 animate in a round as outside one. The opener pays the 2 action points of goal 12's use node only when it is the current participant. | F1 |
| GT49 | walkers: `ag_attempt_move` 5, `ag_attempt_move_near` 61 and 80 (pushed by 3, 4, 7, 8, 33, 52, 55, 56, 68, 79); nodes 0x4272E0 → 0x440700 → 0x43FE00, 0x4284F0 | **Q5 answered.** At the start of each tile step (node 1 → 4 → 5 → 6) node 7 0x4272E0 asks the traversal (0x440700 → 0x43FE00) whether a closed portal lies on the next step, taking the direction from the path or, when path flag 0x1 is set, from the ART, and writes the door into slot 3 (true: 8). Node 8: a closed idle door → **push 11 with the restart bit, delay 50**; otherwise node 9, the walk step 0x42E9B0. So the **same goals 11 → 12 → 70 (→ 72 → 71)** run for the hero and for NPCs: no direct opening, the same 2 action points, point 3 three to four times, point 1, the open sound and the capability test. When 12 pops, the walk resumes at node 1 at once. A locked door ends the whole walk run (GT39); one the walker cannot open is normally refused by the path cost already (LV3). The window branch (node 6 0x427640 → node 11 0x4288A0, push 14) is separate. | F1 |
| GT50 | portal flags field 46 (0x2E) bit 0x100 | **Sticky.** Tested only by 0x42B790: a sticky door ends 70 at its last frame with no 72, so it **stays open** until something closes it. It is no lock bit (LK1–LK13). | F1 |
| GT51 | changes that are no runs; sounds | Script action 88 `toggle_open_closed` (switch 0x44714C, case 0x4495E3 → 0x4EEF80) toggles a portal fully open or closed **at once**: no goal, sound, action points or lock test, and the door's runs are not interrupted (broadcast in multiplayer; the container branch not followed). The Lock event 0x441E90 toggles an open portal closed (LK5); bust 0x4F06E0 destroys a door (LK12); script condition 44 `can_open_portal` (0x4451B0) only calls the attempt. The portal sound 0x4F1010 is called from exactly three places (a full call scan): open at 70's begin, close at 71's begin, locked at 12 node 7. | F1 |

Anomalies the reader suspected, none classified: the end node of 70 is a net no-op, so an interrupted opening or pending close leaves a door open with no close (the condition may be inverted); 72 tests the doorway and then waits 1500 before 71, which never looks again, so a critter entering in that window has the door closed on it; the locked sound is unreachable through the goals (nodes 3 and 4 end first); the pushes of 13 and 15 are dead; a failed 70 start or a refused use script ends the opener's whole run (`end`, not `pop`); the unlock script fires three to four times per open; a Lock event that closes a door while its 70 runs makes the next open step jump back to the first open frame (inference from LK10).

## The S8 read of the executable

Read by one Opus reader in ARC-DEV-139 (no second reader yet) from the same retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52`, the owner's Mac installation) statically with `llvm-objdump` and from the file image (descriptor table 0x5B03D0, goal names 0x5A164C); nothing was copied or run. Behaviour is in prose. GT52 to GT64 answer what GS3, GS9, GS12, GS13, GS31 and SM9 left open for the projectile 36, the spell missile 28 and 78 and the knockback 43 and 64; the notation is that of [the S7 read](#the-s7-read-of-the-executable), with −3 the global 0x5DE6CC (35 ms, set by each tick). Frame slots: 0 the run's object, 1 the target object, 4 the "other" object (shooter, caster or knock source), 5 a location. Established before and only confirmed where touched: PF2–PF14 (flight, crossing order, the end 0x4B2690), TW6, BR1–BR7, IC10–IC14, SM1–SM8, OM15/OM16/OM20/OM24, PS15/PS20. **Slice cut:** ARC-DEV-139 (S8a) makes GT52, GT56–GT58, GT62 and GT64 real for the spell missile; the projectile (GT53–GT55, GT63, B14) is ARC-DEV-140 and the knockback (GT59–GT61) ARC-DEV-141.

Not read: the tick's remaining steps after a boomerang turn within the same tick (BR4's open point; 0x42F6A0 checks the path flag only before its loop); an external destroy of a flying projectile (the kill runs the end node before the destroyed flag is set, so 0x4B2690 would destroy it a second time, inference); the second skip bit of the round-start sweep (goal frame +0x70 bit 0x80) and whether missiles carry it; what the damage context's +0x28 means in 0x4B4390; 0x4514E0's generic goal path (whether any record names 36 or 28) and the record census of AGoal 43; 0x429ED0's false case and 0x42A180's non-positional tests, if any; 0x424D00's art-class exception for hiding; whether knockback runs of critters that are not the current participant step in a round (doors do, GT46: inference that knockback does too); what 0x4AEE50 does with the damage context's +0x30 (the knock source); the rotation-neighbour test's diagonal recursion.

(6 / 7). 6: 0x4269D0 the line to slot 5 (pop / 7). 7: 0x42F5C0 the path node (pop / restart): pace 1000/fps into +0xCFC (unused, the tick uses −3), path flag cleared, step index 0, the range start (frame +0x38) = the projectile's location, run flag 0x10 set. A knockback pushes 64 at once (delay 0) and 64 is current until the run ends, so **during the slide the knockback is a priority-3 run**: a level-3 starter on the critter (the hero's walk or attack click, party orders, dialog start), the round-start sweep, a full-table sweep, a stun (level 4) or knock-down/dying (level 5) end it mid-slide with no end node, leaving the critter on the tile it reached with its pixel offsets. 43's priority 5 counts only at the start and in the AI pre-check (bottom goal > 2 stops the AI tick, GT35). A block puts the object back on the tile it left with offsets 0; a tile change writes the new tile with the same screen point measured from it. The test 0x42FD70: the path flag set means blocked; the tile tested is the **neighbour of the tile left in the line's fixed rotation** (run +0xCD0, 0x4B8FF0; off the map: not blocked), not the tile under the point; terrain 0x4D7110 on it, then walls and portals on the shared edge and the objects of the entered tile through 0x43FD70 with flags 3 (a closed portal blocks with no open attempt, window pieces block, **any living PC or NPC blocks**, other objects unless OF_NO_BLOCK). The "living critter on the last step" branch of the test **never runs** (it needs an index past the last step, and the index is at most len minus 2 during a step), so GT61's slot 3 is never written there. Only the pushed critter takes the damage (the context is built with no attacker and the pushed object as target; the blocker is never hurt): normal into context +0x44, fatigue into +0x54 (the layout of GT31: F1-low), and 0x4B4390 damages +0x20 only. Anomalies the reader suspected, none classified: goal 36 node 4 computes a facing and drops the result; goal 28 node 7 (0x42A200) is unreachable; a knockback is cancelled mid-slide by any level-3 start, including the hero's own click and a round's start (GT62); a spell missile in flight is ended by a round's start and the spell is never released (GT62); the last-step critter branch of the knockback's block test never runs, which is why a critter blocks on every tile change (GT65: likely an oversight of the game; an unreachable branch) and no knockback animation plays; a thrown item whose flight ends through the end node (a map's close, a throw at one's own tile) is placed without the location trigger (GT53, GT63); a failed projectile start on a full table leaves an orphan projectile and a hidden thrown item (GT54, inference). SM9's "latent stale line" does not occur (GT58): the verdict "Original (latent)" is dropped.

## The S9 read of the executable

Read by one Opus reader in ARC-DEV-142 (no second reader yet) from the same retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52`, verified, the owner's Mac installation) statically with `llvm-objdump` and from the file image (descriptor table 0x5B03D0, goal names 0x5A164C); nothing was copied or run. Behaviour is in prose. GT67 to GT82 answer what GS9, GS21, GS31 and §3.5's S9 bullet left open for the eye candy, the "floating" goal, burning scenery and the scenery animation; the notation is that of [the S8 read](#the-s8-read-of-the-executable). Every delay goes through 0x423C80 (the GT17 clock). Descriptor layout used (read for this slice, F1): +0 node count, +4 priority, +8 no-interrupt, +0xC carry, +0x10 bit 0 not counted, +0x14 three aliases, nodes from +0x20 at 32 bytes each (function, two parameter slots and a literal copied by 0x44DD80 into run +0xD00/+0xD08/+0xD10, false step, false delay, true step, true delay), end node at +0x1E0. Frame slot n is frame +8+8n (0x44DD80); slot types (0x5A597C): 0–4 objects, 5–6 locations, 7–19 integers, 20 a handle. Slot 13 (+0x70) holds the frame flags (0x80 = not counted, GT11), run +0xC the run flags, run +0xCFC the stored −2 delay.

**Two corrections to the brief.** (1) Goal 44 `ag_floating` is **not floating text**: it is the hover bob of an object with the spell flag OSF_FLOATING (GT69). Combat/heal floating text is the separate text-floater module (options "text floaters", "float speed", object flag OF_TEXT_FLOATER; code around 0x4D4E20–0x4D5B60), outside the goal table; not read. (2) The scenery restart 0x43F710 does **not** kill then start: it kills only when the object must not animate, and the starter refuses while a main run exists (GT78).

| GT78 | the restart 0x43F710 | Skips a destroyed/off object (field 0x13 & 3) and non-scenery (type 3 only). Scenery flag 0x1 (no auto-animate) → **kill** (0x423FF0) unless a run is being stepped (0x44CB60: 0x5A5978 ≠ −1). Else the art header: more than one frame → start (0x433580); one frame → start when the object has an ambient sound (0x4F1050 kind 2), else kill (same guard). Callers: object flag clear 0x43D280 when OF_OFF is cleared (unless 0x5E2F58), art set 0x43EB30 (every art change), the object-list insert 0x4F2020 (load, placement: `objlist_insert_internal`), and 0x450C40 (an object created by 0x452830/0x4A0470, which first interrupts the scenery at level 5, then restarts: the only "end then start"). An art change on running scenery is **refused** by the starter (main run exists), so the old run keeps stepping with the new art (inference from 0x42DDE0 reading the object's art). | F1 (callers' roles: F1-low) |
| GT79 | the 17 add sites (callers of 0x4CCF70) and 8 remove sites (0x4CD940) | AnimEyeCandy (0x5DE670): 0x433020 (from 0x451AE2, 0x4B122B, 0x4B69CF), a struck critter (types 15/16 only, not 0xDB bit 0x100): index by kind or by damage (>5: 8, ≥10: 9, ≥15: 10), 6/7 by the gore/violence filter 0x5DE6A4, run, limit 3, then 0x459A30. 0x459A30 (three sites, magic-tech table 0x5E7568): per active spell on the struck object index 6·spell+5, fixed 1097, and 1259 for prototype 27369 with no damage. WeaponEyeCandy (0x5DE610): 0x4377C0 two sites, index (weapon prototype − 6029)·5 + k, limit 2, the second (k+5) **queued** under the first (+0x24 bit 0x2) in mode 3. ScriptEyeCandy (0x5E2FB0): 0x44C7A0 add / 0x44C800 remove (script actions). Magic-tech (0x5E7568): AEyeCandy component 0x451FE0 (add or remove by record +0x44; index 6·spell + record +0x40; flags = record +0x48; tag = the run id unless flag 0x100; source → slot 4; not when run +0x13C bit 0x40) and helpers 0x456D20, 0x456E00 (index 6·spell+4), 0x456E60 (code/10·6 + code%10), removes 0x456EC0, 0x45A4F0. Combat (0x5FC1F8): 0x4B80E0 index 0 on a critter, only in a round, removed for a dead or 0x4B7DC0 critter (0x4B7330/0x4B8198 remove; meaning F1-low: a round marker). Trap (0x5FC460): 0x4BC090 (prototype 30000–30007) and 0x4BCB70 (three: index, +1), remove 0x4BC220. Every add builds through 0x44D500: **no site interrupts**, so eye candy runs beside any main run on hero, NPC, scenery, item or trap. None crosses a map by itself; the carry flag lets a carried owner's eye candy cross a hero teleport (GS10). | F1 (the sites' meanings: F1-low where marked) |
| GT80 | interrupts and sweeps (GT8, GT10, GS10, GS21) on these runs | **Eye candy and 44** (no-interrupt): left running at every level below 6 (starts, round start, full-table sweep, stun/knock-down/dying at 4–5, 0x43F1C0's level-5 bust); ended by level 6 (map close, teleport for non-carried owners, a remove of a 45, the stepper's own end) and by the object kill (end node skipped for a killed run, so overlays stay on the dying object). A map's clear runs the end node but 0x410060 makes it do nothing. **1 and 77** (main run, priority 1): ended by any level ≥ 2 aimed at the scenery (the full-table sweep 0x424290(3), 0x43F1C0's level 5 when scenery is busted, 0x450C40's level 5), not by the round-start sweep (not counted); after an end nothing restarts them until one of GT78's restart sites (inference: scenery stops after a table-exhaustion sweep). GT16 holds: every overlay of S9 is started with no interrupt (0x44D500); only 1/77 use 0x44D4E0. | F1 (rule) / inference (situations) |
| GT81 | the light-scheme gate (GT13) | The kill 0x423FF0 used by the restart is the per-object kill with the light-scheme-hour skip (0x4A7FB0). | F2 (GT13) |
| GT82 | text floaters | Not goals: no build of goal 44 or any goal is made for floating text; the module (0x4D4E20, 0x4D5B10–0x4D5B60, settings "text floaters", "float speed") was located but **not read** (kind, colour, duration, rise speed, per-object limit, clock unknown). | F1 (absence in the goal table) / not read |

Anomalies the reader suspected, none classified: an object with OSF_FLOATING but no goal-44 run makes the walk step assert and exit (0x430B74); goal 44 has no end node, so an interrupted bob leaves the object 15 px raised; the remove finds only goal-45 runs, so reverse, callback and fire eye candy cannot be removed by index; 0x4CD9C0 never clears the light entry (stale register at 0x4CDAFF); the ambient sound of goal 1 is decided only at the begin; an art change of running scenery does not restart its run.

Not read: the text-floater module (all of question 3); the eye-candy data (which spells, traps, weapons use which flags; whether any AGoal record names 44); 0x4CDC50/0x4CD7A0/0x4CCDD0 beyond "find free overlay entries" and "already present"; 0x44C7A0's script action numbers; 0x4C0CC0 and the meaning of the combat marker; stat 16 and 0x45DDA0 in the fire damage; 0x4A9650; 0x4B80D0 beyond "round id"; 0x41B930's second argument (loop vs one-shot inferred from use); 0x450C40's identity (object create path); 0x4690D0/0x46A000/0x46B070 writers of scenery flags (editor or prototype parse, not checked); the loader of carried runs (Q1); multiplayer branches.

## The S9b read of the executable

Read by one Opus reader in ARC-DEV-143 (no second reader yet) from the same retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52`, verified) statically with `llvm-objdump` and from the file image; nothing was copied or run. Behaviour is in prose. GT83 to GT93 answer what GT67–GT74 left ambiguous for the eye-candy goals and goal 44, and correct them where noted. Notation: each node is written `false → step, delay / true → step, delay` as stored (false / true), R restart, E end, P pop, −2 the run's stored delay. Frame flags are the run's slot 13, run flags +0xC.

| ID | Subject | Finding | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| GT83 | nodes of 45, 47, 58, 75 (46/48/59/76 are identical in every step, delay and parameter; only the functions differ) | **45:** 1 0x431320 `2 / 3`; 2 step 0x431340 `4 / R,−2`; 3 begin 0x4315B0 `E / R,−2`; 4 finish 0x431A40 `3 / P`. **47:** 2 `4 / 5`; 5 gate 0x42CAA0 `R,−2 / 6`; 6 callback 0x42A260 `4 / R,−2`; the rest as 45. **58:** 3 begin `E / R,0` (stored 0, not −2); 4 callback 0x42A280 `5 / 5`; 5 finish 0x431A40 `P / P`. **75:** 2 step 0x4322A0 `4 / R,−2`; 3 begin `E / 5`; 5 fire 0x42A720 `E / R,−2`. Node 1 answers true when frame flag 0x40 (begun) is **clear**, so true goes to the begin. **Correction:** GT68 has the reverse functions in the wrong order: for 46/48/59 the step is 0x431C40, the begin 0x431E50 and the finish 0x4321C0. | F1 |
| GT84 | the step 0x431340 | Answers true after stepping (and without stepping when the art header fails; the reverse step answers false then). False in four cases: a null object, slots 14–16 all −1, an overlay entry reading −1, a used part already at its last frame **before** the step. The background is stepped after the foreground and can answer false after the foreground advanced. **The action flag (run 0x4) is set when the timing part's new frame equals the ART action frame** (the underlay at slot 15 = −5, else the foreground); GT68's "last frame" is wrong. An action frame of 0 or at or past the count never sets it; the background never sets it; nothing clears 0x4 or 0x8, so a callback eye candy calls back once per run even when looping. **Reverse** (0x431D23, 0x431E00, 0x4325D3) copies the test: 0x4 is set when the frame **before** the backward step equals action − 1 (the new frame is action − 2), and a step from frame 0 answers false first; an action frame of 0 or 1 never fires (looks like an original slip, not classified). | F1 (reverse slip: unclassified) |
| GT85 | the gate and the callbacks | The gate 0x42CAA0 answers true only when 0x4 is set and 0x8 clear, and then sets 0x8. 0x42A260 and 0x42A280 call 0x457000(tag, 3 or 4) and always answer true. 0x457000 does nothing for tag −1 or a free or rejected magic-tech slot; otherwise it writes the phase and processes the run (0x451070, 0x4510F0: the spell's CALLBACK or END-CALLBACK components resume the waiting run; 0x4510F0 not read). | F1 / inference (the spell side) |
| GT86 | the begin 0x4315B0 and the finish 0x431A40 | A null object → false. Sets frame flag 0x40 and stores 100; slots all −1 → true keeping 100 (no re-find). With run flag 0x2000 clear it re-finds the overlay entries (0x4CDC50 → 0x4CCD20 → 0x4CD7A0; a failure → false) and **sets 0x2000, which is never cleared**: a looping eye candy's second begin keeps its places. A header failure → false; else the stored delay is 1000 / rate (unsigned division, a rate of 0 faults the CPU). Start frame: 0, or with run flag 0x800 a random 0..count−1 — **but the forward begin applies it only when the freshly written overlay's frame is non-zero** (0x4317AA…): never at the first begin of an art at frame 0, always at a looping eye candy's second begin (the frame is the last one). The reverse begin starts at count − 1, or with 0x800 a random 1..count − 1. The finish clears 0x40 and answers false for a null object, for looping (frame flag 0x80) and for all slots −1, so 45 begins again; else it clears the overlay fields and the light and answers true (pop). **58/59 never loop**: their finish pops either way, so a looping-flagged 58 pops with its parts still on (whether the pop runs the end node 0x431B20: not read). | F1 |
| GT87 | 75/76 step | 0x4322A0 is 0x431340 plus the fire damage 0x42A720 when the fore overlay's frame **before** the advance mod 3 equals 1, tested before the last-frame test (a last-frame step still burns); the underlay and the background never burn. 0x4324D0 is the same backward. The begin's true leads to node 5 (damage), so the begin burns once. | F1 |
| GT88 | goal 44 | Table: 1 0x431130 (frame flag 0x20 clear → true) `2 / 3`; 2 step 0x431150 `4,100 / R,100`; 3 begin 0x4311F0 `E / R,0`; 4 0x431290 `E / E`. The delays are literal 100. Step false only for a null object, so node 4 never runs. Begin: sets 0x20, counter (slot 14) = 1, delay 100, object offset y −= 15 (x unchanged, 0x43EAF0), restarts at once. Step: counter > 0 → counter + 1 and offset +2, a counter above 5 becomes −1 (the move stays +2); counter ≤ 0 → counter − 1 and offset −2, below −5 becomes 1 (the move stays −2). The offset is written to the **object's own offset field** every step (not draw-only): relative to the unraised object it goes −15 at the begin, then −13, −11, −9, −7, −5, −7, −9, −11, −13, −15 and repeats (ten steps, 100 ms apart, between −15 and −5). The finish (dead) would add 15 − 2·counter and does not restore 0. The walk step (0x4308C9, callback 0x430490) sets a global 15 − 2·counter and writes the object's offset (x, y + global) for every walk node; its sign is opposite to the hover's (suspicious, not settled). | F1 |
| GT89 | the goal choice in the add (0x4CD4BF) | All tests are on the block flags (+0x24), not the entry byte (which gives only 0x20 animated, 0x40 → frame flag 0x80 looping, 0x80 the critter variant). Order: none of 0x18 → 0x1 ? (0x40 ? 76 : 46) : (0x40 ? 75 : 45); else 0x8 → 47, or 48 with 0x1, so **0x8 beats 0x10 and 0x40**; else (0x10) → 58, or 59 with 0x1. 0x200 (ice) adds the frame flag 0x4000 (normal damage) together with 0x40 (mask 0x240); ice alone gives 45/46. **Corrected (GT95):** the add tests the mask 0x240, so either bit sets the frame flag 0x4000 and every 75/76 run does 1 normal damage. | F1 |
| GT90 | the duplicate and the limit | The duplicate test (block bit 0x80, 0x424560) walks the owner object's runs of goal 45 only and compares the tag (slot 11) **and** the normalised art (slot 8, 0x502710). The limit at +0x20 counts the object's field-6 overlay entries of the same normalised art (entries, not runs), stops at the first free entry and refuses only when the count **exceeds** the limit, so it allows limit + 1 copies; the check for back and underlay entries: not read. | F1 |
| GT91 | what Steamwoven models | The run stands for the overlay fields, the light and the end node (the parts are drawn from the table, GT73's clearing is the run's end); the frame, the background's frame, the action and handled flags and the places flag are on the goal frame (the original keeps them in the overlay field and the run flags). The first begin finds the 7/7/4 places of the owner's lists. A rate of 0 or an art that does not read ends the run at the begin (the original faults or answers false). The hover's offset is the host's per-owner sum. | Rule |
| GT92 | mixed-in corrections | GT70's "none of 0x8/0x10 → 45 … with 0x40 → 75/76 …" is the order of GT89. GT72's "step true → 5" for 47/48 and "(6 / restart after −2)" read as `false → restart, true → 6`. GT74's "whenever the fore overlay's new frame number mod 3 is 1" is the frame **before** the advance (GT87). GT69's "an end leaves the object raised" holds: the offset is stored, an interrupt runs no end node. | F1 |
| GT93 | not read | 0x455820 (the magic-tech slot check), 0x4510F0's component dispatch, 0x4CDC50/0x4CD7A0 internals, 0x43EE10's light arguments, whether a pop of the last frame runs the end node 0x431B20, the source of the word ORed at 0x44D887 (run flag 0x800 from block flag 0x20 is inference), the limit check for back and underlay entries, the combat marker and trap sites beyond GT79. | — |

## The S9c read of the executable

Read by one Opus reader in ARC-DEV-144 (no second reader yet) from the same retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52`, verified, the owner's Mac installation) statically with `llvm-objdump`; nothing was copied or run. Behaviour is in prose. GT94 to GT99 settle what GT74, GT76 and GT80 left open about the fire damage, and correct them where noted. Notation as in the S9b read.

| ID | Subject | Finding | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| GT94 | fire damage 0x42A720 | The burning object is slot 0 and is the **attacker** of the combat context (0x4B2210); the source (slot 4, null for goal 77) is only the context's blame copy (+0x30), **GT74 had it the other way round**. The victims are the objects on the burner's own tile (its location, listed by 0x4407C0) of the types in mask 0x1FFE0, which is the types 5..16 (weapon, ammunition, armour, gold, food, scroll, key, key ring, written, generic, PC, NPC): not wall, portal, container, scenery, projectile or trap, not an object that is destroyed or off, and never an item in an inventory (those are in no tile list). Dead critters are listed, and the damage path returns for them. **The burner itself is not excluded**: a 75/76 run on a critter or an item hurts its owner too. With a non-null source a victim is skipped when it is the source or when 0x45DDA0 gives the source (an NPC whose leader chain reaches the source PC); with a null source nothing is skipped and no bonus applies. The base damage is 3 **fire** (damage entry +0x50, type 3) for goal 77 and 1 **normal** (+0x44, type 0) when the frame flag 0x4000 is set; with a non-null source whose stat 16 (magick/tech aptitude, F1-low) is positive it adds stat × 5 / 100 − 1. 0x4A9650(source, victim, 1, 0), the attack event with loudness 1, runs for a non-null source and stops at once for a null one (F1-low). **Round gate:** in a turn-based round each victim is compared with the **run's** stored round id (the frame's slot 19, frame +0xA0, set to 0 by the frame builder): equal means skipped; otherwise both damage entries are doubled and the damage path 0x4B4390 runs. The slot is written once after the loop, with the current round id, and only when at least one victim reached the damage path in a round. So the gate is per run: the first call in a round hurts everyone on the tile once, doubled, and later calls in that round hurt nobody. Outside a round there is no gate: every call (the begin, each restart, each 75/76 step from a frame whose number mod 3 is 1) does undoubled damage. | F1 (stat 16's name and 0x45DDA0's meaning F1-low) |
| GT95 | the frame flag 0x4000 | The add tests the block flags (+0x24) with `test 0x240` at 0x4CD5C4: **either** bit sets frame flag 0x4000. Goals 75/76 are chosen only when 0x40 is set and 0x18 is clear (0x4CD4BF), so every 75/76 run the add builds does 1 normal damage, never 3 fire. GT70's and GT89's "0x200 together with 0x40" is wrong. The 3-fire branch is reached only by goal 77 (0x433580 builds it with frame flags 0 and slot 4 null). A goal number taken from a spell or technology record by the AGoal generic path (0x451646) may be 75, 76, 77 or 1: a data question, not read. | F1 |
