| GT96 | the damage path 0x4B4390 for the burn | For every victim type: the taking-damage script (point 0x17) runs with the context's attacker (the burner) and can veto; a destroyed or invulnerable target (0x400001) exits; resistance 0x4B6860 applies to critters and objects alike, so fire is cut by fire resistance; the hit-point damage is normal + electrical + fire, with the ×1.5 / −¼ difficulty change when 0x40DA20 of the context's attacker is true (a PC burner, meaning F1-low). **Items:** the damage is added; at 0 or less the trap test and trigger run, then the break 0x43F1C0 with the burner as breaker (a first break leaves a busted art, a later one deletes the item, F1-low), so repeated burning destroys an item; no weapon wear (the ordinary-attack flag 0x10000 is never set); witnesses (0x4AEE50) are told only for a non-null source. **Critters:** fire above 0 after resistance sets bit 0x100000, which wears the armour at the context's +0x40 (0, so body armour 1006, F1-low); the experience pool is drained but paid only when the context's +0x30 is a PC; a surviving NPC gets an immediate AI pass (0x4A84F0); a death calls 0x45DA20 with the source as killer (null for goal 77, so no PC experience); no anger or hostility comes from a null source apart from the AI pass. | F1 (the break chain and the null handle F1-low) |
| GT97 | the round id | 0x4B6D70 returns the turn-based-active flag 0x5FC22C (set at 0x4B72D7, cleared at 0x4B734D and 0x4B20C2); 0x4B80D0 returns the round id 0x5FC230, which combat start sets to 0 and 0x4B73A0 then increments, so the first round is 1 and a fresh run's 0 never matches. The stored id survives the end of combat: a run still alive in a later combat skips that combat's round of the same number (kept; the session's `Turn` is the id). | F1 (the survival quirk F1-low, unclassified) |
| GT98 | table exhaustion and the restart sites | The flag 0x5E34F4 is set only at 0x44CD09 (no free run among the 216) and read only at the head of the stepper 0x423560, which calls the sweep 0x424290(3) over every run in use and clears the flag; there is no other handler, and nothing spares or restarts anything. 0x433580 is called only from 0x43F710, whose callers are exactly GT78's four (0x43D400, 0x43EBB9, 0x450E1B, 0x4F2079); neither address is a data pointer, and there is **no per-frame re-check**. The object-list insert 0x4F2020 is reached from the sector load (0x4F1350), the sector fold (0x4F1CD0) and 0x4F1210, so a scenery stopped by a sweep restarts only when its sector is loaded or folded again, an art is set, OFF is cleared or an object is created. **Steamwoven:** the exhaustion sweep stays a rule (§4); the viewer's loops restart through `Start` when the window is shown again, which is the sector-load site, and nothing restarts them in between. The viewer changes no scenery art or OFF flag at run time, so `AreaSceneryHost.Restart` has no caller; it is the rule for the day a script or spell does. | F1 |
| GT99 | not read | Stat 16's name beyond "magick/tech aptitude", 0x45DDA0's use outside this function, 0x4A9650 itself, the break 0x43F1C0 (the busted-then-deleted chain is OD12's), the callers of 0x4F1210, what 0x406CA0 does with a null killer handle, and which spell or technology records name goals 75–77 or 1 in the AGoal generic path. | not read |

## The S10 read of the executable

Read by one Opus reader in ARC-DEV-145 (no second reader yet, so F1 and not F2) from the same retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52`, verified, the owner's Mac installation, `/Applications/Arcanum/Arcanum.exe`) statically with `llvm-objdump`; nothing was copied or run. Behaviour is in prose. GT100 to GT104 settle the tail of the right button's release (RR5) and goal 53, the rotate goal. Notation as in the S9b read.

| ID | Subject | Finding | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| GT100 | rotate starter 0x433440(object, rotation) | In order: a null object answers 0; the art's rotation already equal to the target answers 1 and starts nothing; an object that is not active (0x45D790) answers 1; in a round (0x4B6D70) the object must be the current participant (0x4B6D80) or the starter answers 1; a main run (0x423300, overlays ignored) answers 1. **No movement gate** (0x4339A0 is not called) and no fidget case: a fidgeting critter gets no goal 53 from here. Then it builds goal 0x35 = 53 through 0x44D4E0 (a level-2 interrupt of every run of the object at its priority, GS12; a refusal answers 1), the target rotation in frame slot 14, slot 0 the object, and starts it (0x44D520). Both arms of its second 0x423300 test push the same frame. No action points are charged. | F1 (agrees with GS32) |
| GT101 | the right release's tail, 0x54E146–0x54E228 | Reached after the combat-mode or concealment part and skipped with everything else for a hero that RR2 excludes. **0x424070(PC, level 3, quiet 0, local 0)**: when it answers 0 (a run of priority 4 or 5, such as a spell attempt, refused) the code jumps to the common exit and skips the idle restart, the pointer test and the turn; there is no pre-check of the runs. On success the **idle restart 0x4B4320** is called with no other condition and no action-point test (it starts a fidget only for a type 15 or 16 in combat mode outside a round, or with critter flag 2 bit 0x2). Then the pointer: it is read from the mouse state (0x4FF9F0 polls and copies the block at 0x604690, its +0x1C/+0x20 are the pointer's screen point), converted by 0x4B8730 (screen to tile, RA6; no turn when it answers 0), and 0x5517A0 (the event's x,y over the window under it equals global 0x64C52C, taken as the game view, F0) must hold. The tile test comes before the view test. The facing is 0x4B8D50(the PC's tile, the pointer's tile), the tile-delta rule of RA10 with no angle and no dead zone: the same tile gives rotation 5, so the PC turns to 5 unless it already faces it. Then 0x423300(PC, 0): **no main run** (overlays ignored) goes to the rotate starter (GT100); otherwise, when the first run's bottom goal is the fidget, the art is turned in place (0x502930 builds it, 0x43EB30 sets it and redraws) with no event and no goal; any other main run does nothing. The common exit calls 0x573620 and, when it answers non-zero, 0x575770 and 0x553990 (not read). | F1 (the order and the skip agree with RR5/GS17; the view test F0) |
| GT102 | goal 53 (descriptor 0x5ABFD0), node 0x42C440, end node 0x42C650 | One node, **priority 2**, interrupt allowed, not carried, counted, no aliases. A step answers false (the run ends, delay 0) for a null, stoned (0x10000) or flag-0x60 critter, or an art rotation already equal to the target; in a round, for an object that is not the current participant, true with no change (30 ms). **A fast turn** (0x4B8040: a round with the object as the current participant and either global 0x5FC228 set, taken as the fast turn-based option, F0, or a non-PC that is not in combat mode, not running goal 18 and without NPC flag bit 0x10000000) sets the target at once and ends. Otherwise **one 45-degree step the short way** (diff = current − target: 1..4 → −1, 5..7 → +1, −1..−4 → +1, −5..−7 → −1; the 180-degree tie turns by −1 at +4 and +1 at −4), the art rebuilt (0x502930; when 0x502220 says it does not exist the run ends) and set (0x43EB30), stored in frame slot 8, answer true: restart in **30 ms**. No animation, frame, sound or event changes. The end node snaps a rotation still different from the target to it (unless null, stoned or flag 0x60), so an **interrupted turn still ends facing the target**. Outside a round, with N steps of 45 degrees, the steps are at t0, t0+30, …, the target is reached at t0+30(N−1) and the run ends at t0+30N: 90 degrees: facing done at +30 ms, run over at +60; 180 degrees: +90 and +120 (delays through 0x423C80, GT17). | F1 |
| GT103 | rounds and the network | The tail, the starter and goal 53's nodes call no action-point function and charge nothing. In a round the handler acts only on the PC's own turn (RR1) and the starter has the same participant gate. With local 0 the interrupt of a PC is forwarded to the other side first (GS17); a client's run start asks the host (GS11); the fidget case's art set has no network send in the tail (inside 0x43EB30 not checked). Not followed (R6). | F1 |
| GT104 | not read | Whether 0x64C52C is the game view window (its setter), the meaning of 0x5FC228, the delay of goal 53's first step (taken as the 5 ms of the next pass, GS7, S4), whether a fidget run's frame slot 8 starts at −1 (so whether the turned art survives the fidget's next begin, 0x42CDF0), the exit calls 0x573620, 0x575770 and 0x553990, and whether the screen point needs the game window's origin before 0x4B8730. Mode 3 (Q7) was **not read** in this slice; S10c read it ([GT105–GT113](#the-s10c-read-of-the-executable)). | not read |

## The S10c read of the executable

Read by one Opus reader in ARC-DEV-147 (no second reader yet, so F1 and not F2) from the same retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52`, verified, the owner's Mac installation, `/Applications/Arcanum/Arcanum.exe`) statically with `llvm-objdump`; nothing was copied or run. Behaviour is in prose. GT105 to GT113 answer Q7's mode 3: the held-button walk of the game view's handler 0x54DE50. Notation as in the S9b read.

| ID | Subject | Finding | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| GT105 | what "mode 3" and the held state are | "Mode 3" in GS25 is interface mode 3 (the dialog mode), not a press state or a starter parameter. The tail that marks the run (0x54E6C0–0x54E6DE, the call to 0x436CF0 at 0x54E6CC) sits inside the mode-3 case 0x54E5C5, and mode 0's tile walk jumps into the same tail (0x54DFBF to 0x54E6C0, 0x54DFCE to 0x54E6B9), so the held walk is the same in the normal mode and in the dialog mode. The held state is the global 0x64C6D8: every left-down tile walk or run sets it to 1 at the end of the tail whether the starter succeeded or not; a left up (subtype 1) clears it in mode 0 (0x54E267) and in mode 3 (0x54E6F1), and the interface reset 0x549F00 clears it (its caller not read). Its readers are the tail (0x54E6C0: when it is already 1, call 0x436CF0) and mode 0's object branch (0x54E05E). | F1 |
| GT106 | where the repeats come from | The handler sees a repeat as an ordinary left down (subtype 0); it never reads the repeat marker +0x18. The repeats are made by the polled (DirectInput-style) mouse 0x4FF5A0, reached through 0x4FF390: for each of the three buttons whose state byte (0x604704+i, bit 0x80) is down and was already down, it reposts the button's down subtype (table 0x5BE864: 0, 2, 4) with +0x18 = 1 and resets the button's stamp (0x6046EC+i), once more than 250 ms (0xFA) have passed since the stamp (0x52DFC0 against now 0x739F34): about every 250 ms after the press, as long as the poll runs (its own rate gate 0x604654 not read). The first press posts with +0x18 = 0; the release posts subtype 1, 3 or 5 (table 0x5BE870). The record's point is the pointer's current point (0x6046AC, 0x6046B0), so a repeat walks to the tile under the pointer at that moment, not the first press's tile. The window-message path 0x4FF410 (0x201 left down, 0x202 left up, 0x204/0x205 right, 0x207/0x208 middle, 0x200 move to 0x4FF3B0) has the same 250 ms rule but runs only on button messages, so a held button gives no repeats there (inference). Which path is live depends on 0x62B274 (init flag bit 0x20) and 0x6046B8 (not read). A move (6) does nothing in mode 0 (0x54E24D), a hover (7) goes to 0x54E802, a tick (type 6) only refreshes the hover highlight (0x553A70): none of them walks. | F1 (which path the retail build uses: F0) |
| GT107 | what a press or repeat calls | The same code runs for the first press and every repeat. **Mode 0:** game-view test 0x5517A0, pick 0x552050 (a tile only when out+8 is non-zero), an item armed on the cursor (0x573620) goes to the throw path 0x434F80, which never sets the held flag; then the hero must be active (0x45D790); Shift (keys 0x2A/0x36) does nothing; Ctrl (0x1D/0x9D) or Num Lock (0x45) held with the "always run" option off (0x438D10) starts the run starter 0x433C80, else the walk starter 0x433640 (which itself runs by 0x4304C0 and encumbrance below 4); then the tail. **Mode 3:** the same, except the pick is 0x4F2830 with mask 0x64C680, an object under the pointer does nothing, an armed item does nothing, and the hero must not be dead (0x45D8D0) instead of active. | F1 |
| GT108 | gates of a repeat | They are exactly those of the first press. In a round the handler takes no input unless the PC is the current participant (0x54DE6D–0x54DE85, RR1, TL44), so repeats outside the PC's own turn are dropped. A pointer off the game view or not on a tile: nothing, and the held flag is kept. Shift held: nothing. Armed item: a throw (mode 0) or nothing (mode 3). Inactive hero (mode 0) or dead hero (mode 3): nothing. **An object under the pointer (mode 0):** taken (0x54ED30, GS26) only while the held flag is 0; on a repeat the flag is 1, so dragging over an object does nothing. The starter's own gates run on every repeat: the movement gate 0x4339A0 and the level-3 interrupt 0x424070 (a run of priority 4 or 5 refuses it). A blocked or unreachable tile is the planner's business (GT110). A left release in any mode other than 0 or 3 leaves the flag at 1; the next first press then marks at once, and object clicks in mode 0 are ignored until a release in mode 0 or 3 (follows from the code). | F1 |
| GT109 | which run gets 0x400 | 0x436CF0 passes the 12-byte global 0x5A1908 (initially −1, −1, 0) to 0x436CB0, which sets run flag 0x400 through 0x436D50, in single player only (0x62AC6C bit 0x1 skips it). 0x5A1908 is the last run handle written by a start (0x44D520 writes it as its out pointer) or by a find (0x44E830 writes it on a hit: the object's first run whose goal is the asked goal or one of its aliases). In 0x433640 (goal 3) and 0x433C80 (goal 4), on success it is the hero's walk run, whether found and retargeted, found on the same tile, or newly started. **The first press does not mark** (the flag is 0); **every repeat marks** the same run again, and any later press while the flag is 1 marks too. **Slip:** when the starter fails before its find or start (the movement gate, a refused interrupt, a multiplayer early return), 0x5A1908 is stale: it still names the last run started or found anywhere, possibly another critter's, and that run gets 0x400. A stale handle to a freed run fails in 0x421C30 with the log "turn_on_flags: could not turn animID…". | F1 (the stale-handle case is an inference from the code) |
| GT110 | effect of 0x400 | Planner 0x426040 (goals 3 and 4): run flag 0x400 set makes the first plan use the path flags 0x78 (0x4260C1). When the plan without it fails for a PC (0x40DA20), the planner itself sets 0x400 and plans again with 0x78 (0x426147–0x42618D): LR4, the straight-line plan (every step accepted, critters ignored, no search, the walk stopping at its first refused step, failing beyond 32 tiles). 0x400 also survives the pops between segments (LR4), so a held walk makes every plan of its run a straight line from the second press on; a plain walk gets there only after its first failed plan. Other readers (0x41F4B7, 0x41F97F, 0x41FECD, 0x426C4C, 0x4271ED, 0x42961D, 0x42ECC1, 0x432DFC, 0x434C2E, 0x4258AA) were not read. | F1 (the meaning of 0x78 is LR4's, F2) |
| GT111 | retarget, gait, clearing | A repeat on the run's own target tile changes nothing (0x433800–0x433847) but still marks 0x400 again. On another tile the found run is **retargeted in place**: the replan flag 0x4 is ORed into +0xC00 and the target is written to +0x68/+0x6C (GT14); no restart. The always-run rule (0x4304C0) and the Ctrl, Num Lock and option tests run again on every repeat, so the gait can change mid-hold. The run starter that finds a run sets running 0x40 on it (0x433DEA); whether the walk starter's find on a running run keeps 0x40 depends on the goal aliases (S10b; not re-read). No code in the starters, the retarget or the release clears 0x400; a later plain click that retargets the same run keeps it, and only a new run (after the old one ends) starts without it. | F1 |
| GT112 | left release and rounds | The left release (subtype 1) in mode 0 or 3 only clears the held flag: no interrupt, no stop, no change to the run's flags. **The hero keeps walking to the last target.** In a round the repeats act only on the PC's own turn (GT108). Neither the handler nor the starters read here call an action-point function; what ends a held walk when the action points run out lies in the walk nodes and the round (not read): the input side keeps posting repeats and they are dropped once the turn passes. | F1 (the end by action points: not read) |
| GT113 | other continuous input | In modes 0 and 3 nothing else moves the hero while an input is held. Mode 0 keys (type 5): 0x3B to 0x40 (F1–F6) go to 0x54E534, other keys to 0x54E900 (not read); mode 3's other mouse subtypes go to 0x54E7F0 (not read). | F1 (partial) |
| GT114 | not read | Which mouse path the retail build uses (the setters of 0x6046B8 and 0x62B274) and the poll's own rate gate 0x604654; 0x549F00's caller; the exact meaning of 0x573620 (taken as the armed item, from the throw path); the other readers of 0x400 listed in GT110; the charging of action points and the end of a walk in a round; the alias lists of goals 3 and 4 (taken from S10b); mode 3's other subtypes (0x54E7F0); the key branches 0x54E534 and 0x54E900; whether the modal hook 0x64C6CC (set by 0x553320) blocks the mode-3 tick's hover in practice. | not read |

## The S11 read of the executable

Read by one Opus reader in ARC-DEV-169 (no second reader yet, so F1 and not F2) from the same retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52`, verified, the owner's Mac installation) statically with `llvm-objdump` and from the file image (descriptor table 0x5B03D0, goal names 0x5A164C); nothing was copied or run. Behaviour is in prose. Notation as in the S9b read. Slot 0 is the talker (the hero), slot 1 the target.

| ID | Subject | Finding | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| GT115 | the click's refusals and the talk branch of 0x54ED30 | The refusals come first and are all silent (no message, no sound), in order: no player object (0x40DA50), the hero cannot act (0x45D790), the hero clicks itself, the hero has spell flag 0x10000, the hero has critter flags 0x60. An item armed on the cursor goes to the throw branch. Out of combat mode a target whose first run attacks the hero (0x44E940: the bottom goal 18 or 19, its target compared through leaders, so an attack on a follower counts) puts the hero into combat mode (0x4B3F50), and the click resolves through the combat table as an attack. Otherwise, for a creature (handler 0x54F115): dead without spell flag 0x2000000 gives the loot (goal 81, Alt: the immediate action 0x4445A0); dead with the flag goes on to talk; another player character gives nothing; else **goal 21**. There is no test of hostility, sleep, unconsciousness or the target's combat state, and no Alt or Shift variant. The frame is built by 0x44D500 (slot 1 the target, no tile, no item); talk does not get the flag 0x4000 (uses and loot only). | F1 |
| GT116 | goal 21 `ag_talk` | Descriptor 0x5A81D0: priority 3, no-interrupt 0, 6 nodes, **no end node**. Node 1 (0x42ACD0, false pops): the target is destroyed or off (object flags 0x3), the talker or the target has spell flag 0x10000 or critter flags 0x60, or the target is not a creature: the run ends silently; dead, unconscious, asleep, hostile and in-combat targets are **not** refused here. Node 2 (0x426F10) pushes the get-up 63 when the talker lies fallen; node 3 (0x426E80) pushes the unconceal 54 for an NPC whose art shows animation 5 (never the hero). Node 4 (0x42AE10, the range test): the target must not be destroyed or off, and the distance (0x4B96F0) between the two must be **strictly below the range 0x4AFBB0 gives: 5 for a player character, 10 for anyone else**, so the hero talks from at most 4 cells (matches [NPC AI execution](../research/npc-ai-execution.md)). Node 5 (0x42AF00): the same target tests, the multiplayer gate (a network client does nothing), then 0x460410 calls the interface pointer 0x5E8694 (0x57CC70); it always answers 0, so the run takes the **end step** right after the call (the pop step is dead code). Node 6 (0x42BEA0) writes the literal 1 into the frame's range word and pushes goal 8. When 8 pops, goal 21 runs again from node 1, all checks again. | F1 (the range word's copy into the pushed frame inferred) |
| GT117 | goal 8 `ag_move_near_obj` for the hero | Descriptor 0x5A69D0: priority 2, 6 nodes, end node 0x42B090 (stand animation 0, frame 0). Node 1 (0x425130) pops when the distance is at most the frame's range (1 here) **and** the step toward the target is clear (0x425840); nodes 2 and 3 are the same get-up and unconceal pushes; node 4 (0x425740) pushes the kept path's goal 61 while path flag 0x1 is clear; node 5 (0x427990) is the planner: it does not use the walk planner 0x426040 and never pushes goal 3 or 5, it pushes **61 `ag_attempt_move_near`**; for a target of type 2, 3, 15 or 16 it sets the target's object flag 0x2 (0x43D0E0) so that the target does not block, forces range 0 and plans to the target's tile (flag builder 0x425BF0 with 1, path 0x41F3C0, budget 0x426320), clears the flag (0x43D280) and drops the path's last step, so the hero ends on an adjacent cell. A plan that fails ends a **non-PC's** subturn (0x4B7C90), not a PC's. Node 6 (0x4246D0) is the common no-path node: out of a round the pause goal 6 waits a second and ends the whole run, in a round the run ends at once; no retry inside the run. No message and no sound in goals 21, 8 and 6. | F1 |
| GT118 | a target that moves, a blocked step | Goal 61 node 5 (0x4254C0) compares the target's location with the one stored when the path was planned; a change sets path flag 0x4 and, with flag 0x4 or 0x8, the path is reset (0x430FC0) and 61 pops to goal 8, which tests the range and plans again (the tail after the reset partial). A blocked step (61 node 10, 0x433270) charges 2 action points (not for the local PC) and pops to goal 8 for a new plan. Goal 8 never sets or clears the running flag. | F1 (partial) |
| GT119 | the start branch for a talk | Confirms GS27: with auto attack on an equal goal 21 already queued (0x44E6F0: the same parameters, the same NPC) makes the click ignored, otherwise the hero is interrupted at level 3 and the run starts; with it off, outside a round, an equal goal queued gives the level-3 interrupt and a new start, so a repeated talk click **restarts** the run and the approach is planned again; in a round the click is taken only while the hero's run is its fidget at depth below 4. Afterwards, in single player, Shift sets 0x100 (0x436C50); otherwise running is turned on. | F1 |
| GT120 | what the last node's interface call does | 0x57CC70(talker, target): first the script attachment point 9 (dialog) on the target, triggered by the hero (0x441980); anything but "run default" ends the request (the script's dialog action has run, even for a fighting NPC). On the default, a surrendered target (danger state 3, 0x4AF210) whose source is in the hero's party makes the NPC flee from the hero (0x4AF130) and nothing opens. Otherwise the **generic greeting** 0x5681C0 is a floating line, not the window: can-speak 0x4AD800 with flag 0 refuses with an empty line (no "busy" text), the NPC's text bubble is replaced, and an NPC that is alive with no main run is interrupted at level 3 and **turned toward the hero** (0x441B20), even when the line is empty. The **window** opens only through the script's dialog action and the start callback 0x567460, which refuses silently a dead or unconscious hero, a can-speak answer other than 0 (2 combat focus or flee, 3 busy, 4 another player character's conversation, 5 and 6 off, stunned, mute, asleep, unconscious), the local hero in interface mode 3 and a hero in combat mode that may not leave it; otherwise it leaves combat mode (0x4B3F20), and the dialog start 0x412FD0 can end at once on kill-on-sight. After the window comes up it interrupts the hero's runs at level 3 (0x424070, which includes the talk run itself), turns the **hero** toward the NPC and unconceals both; the NPC is not turned by this path. No distance re-check in 0x42AF00, 0x57CC70 or 0x567460. Can-speak with flag 0 also calls 0x424070(npc, 2), which ends the NPC's priority-1 and -2 runs; a priority-3 run that refuses gives "busy". So no dialog opens for a target in combat or fleeing. | F1 |
| GT121 | the dialog's end, interrupts | The interface's end (0x5678D0) closes the window, frees the dialog state (0x412F40), expires the NPC's bubble (0x4D6160) and ends the reaction slot (0x413280); **no goal is started or resumed** for the NPC or the hero. During the dialog the NPC's AI pass stops at the gate's conversation-partner step (CL row 5; not re-read); if its danger state leaves none or surrender the gate's UI callback ends the conversation (this is the AI host's `ConversationCallback` hold-out of [clocks-frame](clocks-frame.md), still open). Any interrupt ends the whole talk run, calling the end nodes top-down: 61's 0x430F20 (stand art, frame 0, path flag; skipped for a killed run), 63's 0x42B090 and 54's 0x42AFB0 when on the stack, 8's 0x42B090; goal 21 has none. Nothing touches the NPC before node 5, so an interrupted approach leaves the NPC alone. Level 3 ends a talk run (priority 3; walks 2): a new click, the right release and a round's start sweep; the levels of a stun and of the windows were not read. | F1 (partial) |
| GT122 | anomalies | Not classified: a **dead target is not refused by goal 21** (a target that dies during the approach ends in an empty greeting that still clears the NPC's bubble and turns nothing, inferred); a busy or fighting NPC gives no message, only an empty line, and may still be turned and have its walk ended; two ranges (a hero 5 cells away walks to an adjacent cell, one at 4 talks where it stands) are the node design, not a slip; a **Shift** talk click on a far NPC (flag 0x100: the walk step answers 0) may loop goal 8 → 61 → node 10 → pop → plan with no end until another order (inferred, not traced; worth a second reader); node 5's pop step and 0x54ED30's read of the target's field 1 are dead code. | F1 |
| GT123 | not read | What 0x5E8694 holds at run time (taken from GS29); the empty-string handling of 0x4D5FE0; the second-stage planner branch of 0x427990 (0x427E2A and after); the levels used by the stun and window paths; the hero's action points per walk step; whether the frame push copies the range word +0x40 (inferred); what follows the NPC's side after the dialog (the next AI pass, not read). | not read |

## The ARC-DEV-170 read of the executable

Read by one Opus reader (ARC-DEV-170, B06, no second reader yet, so F1) from the same retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52`, verified, the owner's Mac installation) statically with `llvm-objdump`; nothing was copied or run. Behaviour is in prose. The full row is [ledger WS14](../research/ledger/npc-ai.md#npc-waypoints-and-standpoints-arc-dev-117); these rows are what the goal table needs of it.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Level | Steamwoven |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| GT124 | goal 7 (`ag_move_near_tile`): descriptor 0x5A67D0, starter 0x4341C0, nodes 0x424D90, 0x426F10, 0x426E80, 0x425740, 0x427730, 0x42C610, 0x4246D0 | Goal 7 has the nodes of goal 3 except node 5 (the planner 0x427730 for 0x426040). The run ends on the **exact** destination tile (node 1 compares the location with slot 5 in both dwords); the starter's third argument (the waypoint step's 3, the standpoint step's 1) is stored in slot 7 and **no node reads it**: the goal name's "near" is not a range. The planner tries the path with request flag 0 and then with flag 1 (a step onto the destination skips the blocking tests, so a path may end on an occupied tile). A new run is built through 0x44D4E0 (an interrupt at goal 7's priority 2) and is a plain walk. | F1 (one reader) | `AiExecutorRules` interprets goal 7 with the run-to-tile node (`RunToTileNode`), the gait from the run's flags (none: walk); the frame's range slot holds the mode and is never read. |
| GT125 | walk-to starter 0x4341C0 | Movement gate 0x4339A0 first; then the finder for goal 4 (aliases 7, 3, 55): a match with the same tile changes nothing, with another tile ORs the replan flag and writes the tile (the mode is not rewritten, the run keeps its goal and gait: a retargeted run or goal 55 stays a run); no match starts goal 7 as above. Answers 1 on success, 0 on a failed gate or a refused interrupt. | F1 | `AreaAiHostRoutes.StartRouteWalk` (retarget through `GameSessionOwner.RetargetGoal`, else `StartGoal` with `GoalStarters.Generic`; the movement gate is `StartGatePasses`, which now lists goal 7). |
| GT126 | not read | The full body of the path builder 0x41F3C0 and of 0x426500/0x426560, what 0x4C1110 means in the movement gate, the network branches of 0x4341C0, and what sets run flag 0x4000. | — | — |

## The S11b read of the executable

Read by one Opus reader in ARC-DEV-174 (no second reader yet, so F1 and not F2) from the same retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52`, verified, the owner's Mac installation) statically with `llvm-objdump` and from the file image (descriptor table 0x5B03D0, goal names 0x5A164C); nothing was copied or run. Behaviour is in prose. Notation as in the S7 read (the new step word 0x70000000+N is "pop, then push N": the goal is replaced in the same frame and the frame is not copied). Slot 0 is the user (the hero), slot 1 the target.

| ID | Subject | Finding | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| GT124 | goals 38 and 81, descriptors and nodes | Both: priority 3, no-interrupt 0, 6 nodes, no aliases, **no end node**, one shared node table. Node 1 (0x428930) tests only that both handles are not null, and writes the frame's range word (+0x40): **1 for a container (type 2), a player character (15) or an NPC (16), 2 for every other type** (scenery, portal, wall, item, trap). Node 2 (0x425130, the same function as goal 8's node 1) passes when the distance (0x4B96F0, Chebyshev) is **at most the range** and the access is clear (0x425840: the target's own tile not terrain-blocking for the user, the step toward it not blocked by an object, the target itself ignored), else pushes goal 8. The push copies the whole frame from below, so goal 8 inherits the range (settles GT123's question). When 8 pops, the goal runs again from node 1. There are **no get-up or unconceal nodes** (a fallen user in range uses lying down, inferred). Node 3 (0x4284A0) is true for a portal and goes to node 5 (0x42BE50 copies the target into the door slot) and node 6 (0x4284F0: a portal that is not open and has no main run is replaced by goal 11 open door, otherwise by 49 close door); anything else goes to node 4. The only creator of 38 and 81 is 0x54ED30. | F1 |
| GT125 | the use node 0x428A10 | Charges 2 action points first (0x4B7CD0: only in a round, only for the critter whose turn it is; with too few the local PC with AP and fatigue above 1 takes 2 fatigue damage and the use goes on, anyone else ends the subturn and the run). **The node does not turn the user** (this corrects GS29): it runs the access test 0x425840 again and ends the run without the use when it is blocked (after the AP were paid). Then by the target's type: **a container**: the loot window for the local player only (0x4EE310 → 0x57CB10; an NPC pays the AP and nothing happens); **a creature**: no loot when the user has spell flag 0x20000 or the target has a main run (a corpse whose death run goes on), there is **no dead test**, otherwise as a container; **scenery**: art scenery type 22 (a bed) calls the rest window 0x460820, any other scenery falls through; **everything else (including a portal that got here)**: the use script (point 1, triggerer the user, attachee the target, 0x441980). The script's answer is **ignored**: both outcomes end the run; the engine has no default action after it. A scenery or portal with no script at point 1 does nothing, silently. | F1 |
| GT126 | traps and the loot window | Inside 0x441980 for point 1: a user that has not spotted the target's trap yet makes a spot attempt (0x4BBFE0, 0x4BBE40); a successful spot marks the trap and **skips the script**, so the use is lost; a failed spot or an already spotted trap runs the script, so the trap fires (inferred from the call order). For a container or a corpse the use script runs **inside the loot window's open** (0x572370), not in the goal: it refuses a null, dead or non-PC user, **turns the hero toward the target** (the only turn of the whole use path), refuses silently when the sight test 0x4AF260 answers non-zero, tests the lock for a container (0x4AED80: the unlock script point 3, jam and magic-hold bits of field 0x38, the lock and the key; a failure shows its message and plays the target's sound 2), then runs point 1 and any answer but "run default" refuses the open with the same sound; an empty corpse gets a UI message (line 2000 of a message file, not read). | F1 (message texts not read) |
| GT127 | head checks and silent ends | Node 1 tests no destroyed or off target, no user flags 0x60 or 0x10000: those are the click's refusals (GT115), which also refuses 38 and 81 when the hero has spell flag 0x20000 (0x54F05F, 0x54F07D). Locked, jammed and held states are tested only in the loot window's open or by the door goals. Silent ends: a null handle, the AP failure, a blocked access, a corpse with a main run, user spell flag 0x20000, a spotted trap. A blocked target tile is never in range; goal 8 then fails to plan and ends the whole run through goal 6 (inferred). | F1 |
| GT128 | interrupts | Goals 38, 81, 11, 49, 12, 50 and 6 have no end node; goal 8's is 0x42B090 (GT121). A level-3 interrupt (priority 3) ends the run and calls only those. The 2 AP are charged only inside node 4, so an interrupt before it costs nothing, and **no code refunds AP**. | F1 |
| GT129 | map exits | **Jump points are not uses**: they fire on a location change (0x444270 calls `map_process_jumppoint` 0x4101D0 at 0x4443DE, for player characters, on the new tile, TR3). **Script exits are uses**: clicked scenery goes through goal 38 with range 2, node 4, then the point-1 script with the hero as the triggerer; the cave entrance's art is scenery type 20, not the bed 22. Steamwoven's use of a trigger object at range 2 matches the range word. The original's goal 8 plans scenery (type 3) to an adjacent cell (GT117: range forced to 0, last step dropped), though the use is allowed at distance 2. | F1 |
| GT130 | anomalies and not read | Not classified: the AP are paid before the access re-test (probably unreachable, since node 2 ran the same test); an NPC pays the AP for a container or corpse and nothing happens (latent: NPCs never get 38 or 81 from a click); no dead test in the creature branch (a revived corpse would be looted alive); a portal never gets the point-1 script through goal 38; a spotted trap still fires on use (inferred, probably design). Not read: the meaning of spell flags 0x20000 (user) and 0x10000 (loot target) and object flag 0x400; the message line 2000 and the lock messages; what 0x4B7690 shows; goal 8's planner branch for portals and types outside 2, 3, 15, 16 (0x427E2A on); whether nodes 2 to 4 run in one stepper call; the multiplayer interrupts; who sets the script-disabling global 0x5E2F58. | not read |

## The S11c read of the executable

Read by one Opus reader in ARC-DEV-175 (no second reader yet, so F1 and not F2) from the same retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52`, verified, the owner's Mac installation) statically with `llvm-objdump`; nothing was copied or run. Behaviour is in prose. Slot 0 is the picker (the hero), slot 1 the item.

| ID | Subject | Finding | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| GT131 | goals 15 and 16, descriptors | Goal 15 `ag_pickup_item` (descriptor 0x5A75D0): priority 3, no-interrupt 0, 10 nodes, no aliases, **no end node**. Goal 16 `ag_attempt_pickup` (0x5A77D0): priority 3, no-interrupt 0, 2 nodes, no aliases, **no end node**. | F1 |
| GT132 | goal 15's nodes | Nodes 1, 2, 4 and 5 are constants (0x429370 is 0, 0x429380 is 1, 0x4293A0 jumps to node 10). **Node 3** (0x424D90, args: the picker, slot code 0x20) compares the picker's location with the item's: equal pushes goal 16, otherwise node 4. **Node 10** (0x42BEA0) copies its argument 0 into the frame's range word (+0x40) and pushes goal 8 (which inherits the range 0 from the pushed copy). Nodes 6 to 9 (goals 20 `ag_kill` and 17 `ag_pickpocket`) are unreachable: compiled-out ownership checks. Slot code 0x20 resolves to the item's own location, or, for a carried item, to the **holder's** location (and rewrites the item's location field). There is **no head check**: no destroyed or off test, no picker flags; only goal 8 and goal 16 look at the item. When goal 8 pops, goal 15 runs again from node 1. | F1 |
| GT133 | range: the hero walks onto the tile | Goal 8 with range 0 pops (node 1, 0x425130) only on the same tile (Chebyshev distance at most 0) with the access clear. Its planner 0x427990 makes one plan to the exact tile for an item (an item is none of the special types 2, 3, 15, 16, so the target is not made non-blocking and no last step is dropped); a failure goes to the no-path node 6 (GT117). A hero already standing on the item's tile pushes goal 16 at once. **The hero is no different from the NPC here.** | F1 |
| GT134 | the click and the starter | Item click, 0x54ED30: hero spell flag 0x20000 refuses it silently (both modes); in combat mode the click always builds goal 15 (Alt is not tested); out of combat mode Alt calls the immediate action 0x4445A0 (not read), otherwise goal 15. The frame comes from 0x44D500 (slot 0 the hero, slot 1 the item, no tile). There is no run flag 0x4000 for goal 15 (it is set for 38 and 81 only); the start branch is GS27's. The starter 0x4356C0 is called only by the NPC search (0x4A9090, 0x4A928E). | F1 |
| GT135 | goal 16 node 1 and the take | Node 1 (0x42A9B0) answers 0 for null handles or an item with object flags 0x3, otherwise calls 0x42AA70; both outcomes go to node 2 (0x42BD40: stand art, animation 0 frame 0), which takes the **end step: the whole run ends, goal 15 included**. There is no turn and no pickup animation. 0x42AA70 in order: both handles non-null; the global switch 0x4681A0 (0x5E8800) on (it applies to **every** picker, the hero included: while it is 0, with an open container window, nothing is taken); an item with a parent must be in a junk pile (description 3023), otherwise it fails silently with no AP; **1 action point** (0x4B7CD0) for every take, from the ground or from a pile (only in a round, only for the critter whose turn it is; with none the turn ends and nothing is taken); the local player's pickup sound at the picker's location **before the transfer is tested**; a multiplayer client returns 1 without moving anything; then the move 0x4617F0 → 0x461810. | F1 |
| GT136 | the move 0x461810 | An item with a parent goes to 0x461CA0 (not read). A ground item first runs script point 4 (0x441980, the picker triggers, the item is attached; any answer but "run default" refuses silently; point 4 is probably the get point). Then the test 0x466510: code 1 too heavy (carried weight plus the item's weight above stat 8, inferred the carry limit; a key is exempt for a picker with a key ring), code 2 no room (no matching stack and no free grid cell), code 15 (item flag 0x80000000 with 0x4C2690 answering 0x48). A refusal shows a UI message (line 100 plus the code of the item message file, type 4, to the local player only) for a player character, floating text for an NPC. On success the item leaves the map (0x4417A0), is inserted at the slot the test chose (0x466640, not read: a matching stack is probably merged) and its queued Item Decay event (kind 25) is removed (0x468110, unless 0x468010 answers non-zero). | F1 (texts, 0x466640, 0x461CA0 not read) |
| GT137 | interrupts, the loot, anomalies, not read | Goals 15 and 16 have no end node; a level-3 interrupt ends the run through the pushed goals' end nodes only (goal 8's 0x42B090, GT121). The AP, the script and the transfer all happen in one node-1 call, so an interrupt cannot fall between them, and **no code refunds AP**. A creature click never builds goal 15; nothing contradicts GT115, GT125 or GT126 for a dead creature or a container (goal 81, S11b). **Anomalies (not classified, medium confidence the first is a game bug):** the AP and the sound come before the script and the weight/room test, so a refused take still costs 1 AP in a round and still sounds for the local player; the hero is subject to the NPC pickup switch 0x4681A0 (probably latent); goal 15 has no target check, so an item taken during the walk resolves to the holder's tile (inferred: goal 8 then fails and ends through node 6; goal 16 would refuse as the parent is no pile). **Not read:** 0x4445A0 (Alt), 0x461CA0 (the pile path, whether an emptied pile is destroyed), 0x466640 (insert or merge), 0x4417A0 beyond its start, 0x468010, the message texts (lines 101, 102, 115), the meaning of 0x4C2690 = 0x48 and of stat 8, whether the access test 0x425840 passes at distance 0, the sound table of 0x4F0BF0, the multiplayer packet path (0x49DD4E also calls 0x42AA70). | F1 |

## The S11e read of the executable

Read by one Opus reader in ARC-DEV-177 (no second reader yet, so F1 and not F2) from the same retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52`, verified) statically with `llvm-objdump`; nothing was copied or run. Behaviour is in prose. Node notation: a node is written "function (on 0 / on non-zero)".

| ID | Subject | Finding | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| GT138 | the callback global 0x5F848C | The AI module init 0x4A8320 sets 0x5F848C (and its sibling 0x5F8488) to 0; the setter 0x4A84D0, called once from the dialog UI's init (0x567377), stores 0x5F848C = 0x5678D0 (the dialog end) and 0x5F8488 = 0x568430. So the callback is set once at start-up and stays set: it does not depend on a dialog being open or on its kind. Steamwoven's former `ConversationCallback == false` was wrong. | F1 |
| GT139 | what the callback does | 0x5678D0(PC, 0) finds the PC's dialog slot (0x567420: in single player always slot 0, whatever handle is passed); if the slot is inactive it does nothing. Otherwise it clears the active word; for the local PC it pops the interface stack one level (0x553350 → 0x551A80), which closes dialog mode 3; then (not for a network client) it frees the dialog state (0x412F40), expires the NPC's bubble (0x4D6160) and calls 0x413280 → 0x4C10A0, which writes the NPC's reaction slot 0 back to null: that slot (NPC field 0x129, index 0) is what the gate reads as the conversation partner (0x4C1110), so the end clears the partner. No goal, turn or message for the hero or the NPC (agrees with GT121). **Barter:** when another mode is pushed over mode 3 the stack calls 0x567A20 instead (the dialog is suspended: the active word is 0, the window closed, the state and the partner kept) and popping back resumes it (0x567A60); while a window sits over the dialog the callback is a no-op and does not close the barter or any other mode (inferred from the stack code; the barter's own push was not read). | F1 (partly inferred) |
| GT140 | who calls it | The AI gate (0x4A8570, 0x4A861D–0x4A8657): after the stops for a dead NPC, a hidden or switched-off one, the PC's object flag 0x2 and the NPC's spell flag 0x10000, the partner is 0x4C1110(npc) (non-null only for an NPC); if it equals the local PC, the callback is set and the danger state (frame +0x10) is neither 0 nor 3, the gate calls callback(PC, 0): the same as SchedulerRules' CombatFocus/Flee. In every partner case the gate then returns 0: the NPC does not act in that pass; it stops being held from the next one only if the callback ran (the danger state is neither 0 nor 3), otherwise it stays held. A second reader, 0x4A9AD0(critter, other), calls the callback unconditionally when the two differ and puts the critter into combat mode (and may start an attack): its callers are the attack event 0x4A9650 at 0x4A986F when the victim is a PC and the event's flag 0x2 is clear (**an attack on the PC ends the PC's dialog**) and a combat-hit helper at 0x4B27D7 (condition: field 0x4D bit 0x1000; not identified). Nothing ends a dialog when the NPC dies, is stoned or falls asleep (the gate's stops come first, so such an NPC is held), nor when a round starts (inferred; searched through the callback's users only). The dialog UI itself calls 0x5678D0 at its choice and script ends (0x567ECB–0x56885F, not read). | F1 (partly inferred) |
| GT141 | pick pocket is goal 33, not 17 | The steal 0x575180 (→ 0x4C6FD0) and the plant 0x575100 (→ 0x4C7010) call 0x4350F0(thief, victim, item, skill 5, flag 0 or 1), which builds goal 33 `ag_use_skill_on`; goal 17 `ag_pickpocket` (0x5A79D0) is a one-node wrapper (0x42BE80 with 5, then a push of 33) reachable only from goal 15's dead nodes (GT132). Starter 0x4350F0: skill 14 goes to 0x436220; the gate 0x4348E0(thief, 4) needs a thief that can act (outside a round that is enough; in a round only the critter whose turn it is may start, an NPC also needs 4 AP, the PC no minimum); victim in frame slot +0x10, item +0x20, skill +0x68, the flag ORed into +0x70; running on through 0x436AB0 (the PC when 0x4304C0 allows it). | F1 |
| GT142 | goal 33 | Descriptor 0x5A97D0: 7 nodes, priority 3, no-interrupt 0, end node 0x42B090. Node 1 (0x42BEA0) writes range 1; node 2 (0x425130) tests distance at most the range and the access; node 3 (0x425740) and node 4 (the planner 0x427990, goal 5 pushed) walk it: **goal 33 never pushes goal 8**, it plans itself and walks with goal 5; node 6 is the no-path node 0x4246D0 (end, or the wait 1000); node 5 (0x42A490) replaces the goal by 67 (lock picking) for skill 14, otherwise node 7 (0x42A440) replaces it by **86 `ag_attempt_use_pickpocket_skill_on` for skill 5**, else by 34. There is no sight test in goals 33 or 86. | F1 |
| GT143 | goal 86 | Descriptor 0x5B01D0: 9 nodes, priority 3, end node 0x42B090. Node 1 (0x42CA90) tests run flag 0x10 (clear: node 3; set: node 2, 0x42CC80, an animation-in-progress branch; who sets 0x10 not read). Node 3 (0x42C0F0) turns the thief toward the victim (refuses a null handle, thief spell flag 0x10000, thief critter flags 0x60). Node 4 (0x42A2A0), the PP11 node: victim object flags 0x3 refuse, then **4 AP** (0x4B7CD0), then the check 0x4C7090 → 0x4C7160 (the plant adds invocation flag 0x2); the check's success bit sets run flag 0x40000 (a fate point sets it too). Node 5 (0x42A430) reads it: **on success no animation** (node 8: animation 0, then pop, the run ends), **on failure** animation 12 (node 6), the wait for it (node 7, 0x42CB10) and node 9 (0x433270), which charges 2 more AP to anyone but the local PC; then the run ends. The AP come after the turn and before the check. | F1 |
| GT144 | the skill-bar press, anomalies, not read | 0x57A320 sends skill 5 to its own branch: PC only, the refusals of PP1/PP9, then 0x57A710 opens the window; **it starts no goal and no walk**. After a drag that queued the goal the steal-mode drop code clears the dragged item and redraws both inventories (0x466260) and does not close the window (inferred; the close path was not traced), so the window stays open while goal 33 walks. If the victim moves, goal 5 follows the old path and goal 33 replans when it pops (inferred). No node of 33 or 86 tests a dead or stoned victim: a victim who dies during the walk is still robbed (inferred, a latent game bug). An NPC thief pays 6 AP on a failure and 4 on a success, the PC always 4. A dead-victim or moved-victim window close, whether a click elsewhere or the right release closes the steal window, the meaning of field 0x4D and the identity of 0x4B2690, 0x42CB10, 0x42CC80 and 0x42BE80 in detail, the barter's mode push, multiplayer, and 0x568430 were **not read**. | F1 (partly inferred) |

## The S13b read of the executable

Read by one Opus reader in ARC-DEV-188 (no second reader yet, so F1 and not F2) from the retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52`, verified) statically with `llvm-objdump`; nothing was copied or run. The spell data came from the installed `rules\SpellList.mes` and `mes\spell.mes`. Behaviour is in prose.

| ID | Subject | Finding | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| GT145 | which effects hold | Three flags hold a critter: **Paralysed** (critter flag 0x40, field 0xDA), **Stoned** (spell flag 0x10000, field 0x14) and **Entangled** (spell flag 0x1000000, field 0x14). Stunned (0xDA 0x20) is the damage path's goal 57 (GT28) and is not a hold. Object flag 0x100 "don't draw" on a PC or NPC also sets 0xDA 0x40. In the spell data: Entangle (spell 51) sets ENTANGLED at Begin and clears it at End (maintained, adds Effect 62); Stasis (78) sets PARALYZED (maintained); Quench Life (59) sets it at Begin and clears it in its eye-candy callback; the mastery Paralyze (113), Stasis (133), Freeze (134) and several tech and item entries also set PARALYZED; Flesh to Stone (48) and mastery 127 set STONED. Charm, fear and sleep do not hold. | F1 |
| GT146 | setting the flag (ObjFlag component 0x454A10, mode 1) | The reference count 0x455100 first: when another active run already provides the bit, nothing happens. For 0xDA mask 0x40 and for 0x14 mask 0x1000000 the component calls the interrupt-by-goal routine 0x44E4D0 for goal 3 and for goal 61 with the author −1 (level 6), then writes the field. Stoned (0x14, 0x10000) only sets the render flag 0x80000 and interrupts no goal. "Don't draw" on a PC or NPC ORs in 0xDA 0x40 directly without the interrupt pair. No goal is started (no 57, no knock-down), the turn does not end, there is no PC/NPC branch. | F1 |
| GT147 | interrupt-by-goal 0x44E4D0(obj, goal, by) | With by −1 the level is 6. It walks the owner's runs and interrupts any run whose **bottom** frame or **current** frame has that goal type. **Ended:** a walk (goal 3 at the bottom) and any run whose current goal is 61 (goal 8 move-near pushes it at nodes 4/5). **Not ended:** run 4, run-near 55 and 56, move-near 7 and a skill walk 33, because their current goal while stepping is 5; attack, cast and skill runs whose current goal is not 61 are not ended either. | F1 (which pushes reach 61 from 18, 79 and 7: not read) |
| GT148 | the step start 0x42E9B0 (goal 5 node 9; also goals 61 and 80) | False when run flag 0x100 is set; true when a step is already begun (frame flag 0x20); false for a stoned object and for a PC or NPC with 0xDA & 0x60. Node 9's false answer is "restart" with delay 0, so goal 5 loops in place and the run stays alive. The tile move already under way tests no hold flag, so that tile completes. Goals 3, 4 and 5 contain no other test of 0x60, stoned or entangled. **A paralysed or stoned runner freezes in place but keeps its run; when the flag clears it resumes toward the old destination.** The step start never tests entangled. | F1 (the restart's real timing: not read) |
| GT149 | the plan for an entangled critter | The path request set-up 0x425BF0 answers 0 for a PC or NPC with spell flag 0x1000000, so the path builder is not called (callers: the planners of goals 3, 4, 68, 7, 55, 8, 33, 56, 52 and the wander nodes). An entangled critter cannot plan a **new** path but can step along one it already has. | F1 |
| GT150 | gates for new orders | Movement gate 0x4339A0 (can act 0x45D790: alive, conscious, 0xDA & 0x60 clear, spell flag 0x10000 clear; in a round the current participant); start gate 0x4348E0 also asks can-act; the tile click and the held-stick repeats (GT107) ask can-act; the object click 0x54ED30 silently refuses stoned or 0x60 (GT115); the combat-mode toggle 0x4B3F80 tests 0x40 and 0x20 (modelled). A paralysed or stoned PC's clicks and repeats are dropped silently. **Entangled is not in can-act**: an entangled PC's click passes every gate, a new walk starts and its plan fails (GT149), which leads to the no-path node. A held repeat retargets a found run and sets its replan flag; that the replan then ends the run through the failed plan is inferred. | F1 (0x54E0AF–0x54E0CD not read) |
| GT151 | other running goals | The attack swing begin 0x432990, the turn 0x42C0F0 (goals 19, 34, 67, 86), the cast node 0x42C850 and the animation step and begin (0x42CC80, 0x42CB10, outside the lying animations) refuse 0x60. Rotate 53 ends. The spell attempt 27 has no flag test, so a cast under way finishes (inferred). In a round a participant that cannot act (paralysed or stoned, not entangled) has its turn skipped (0x4B7580, GT27). | F1 |
| GT152 | how the hold ends | The spell's End (Entangle, Stasis) or callback (Quench Life) runs the component in mode 0: the reference count falls to at most 1 and the bit is cleared. **No goal starts: no get-up, no animation reset.** The frozen run of GT148 resumes. The AI tick never cures 0x40 (GT35). Clearing "don't draw" on a PC or NPC clears 0xDA 0x40 unconditionally. | F1 |
| GT153 | is B11 the original's own behaviour? | **Partly yes**, inferred from the absence of tests (not seen in play): (a) an entangled hero already running (goal 4, 55 or 56) keeps running to the end of the planned path; only a new order (a failed plan) stops him; (b) a paralysed or stoned hero freezes after the current tile and resumes running to the old target when the hold ends, with no new click. A walk (goal 3) is ended at once by paralysis and entangle, not by stone (which interrupts nothing, GT146). | F1 (inferred) |

Not read: what Effect 62 (Entangle) does; goal 5 node 5 (0x425430) and the replan path after a retarget while entangled; the real time of the delay-0 restart; the PC's spell-cast start (the invocation check 0x4564E0 has no 0x60 test at its first call level); the branch at 0x54E082; 0x435D70, 0x460C30 and the reference count's internals (0x458CF0); which goals push 61 from 18 and 79; what a frozen run looks like on screen; a second reader (F2).

## The S14 read of the executable

Read by one Opus reader in ARC-DEV-231 (no second reader yet, so F1 and not F2) from the retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52`, verified) statically with `llvm-objdump`; nothing was copied or run. The action numbers are the catalogue types; the executor 0x447220 reads the jump table 0x44ADBC at entry type − 1 (consistent with 24 → 0x447822 and 71 → 0x448E25). **Two names of GS30 were wrong:** action 76 is *heal fatigue* (it starts get-up only as a side effect) and action 115 is *try to steal 100 coins*.

| ID | Subject | Finding | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| GT154 | what every case returns | Each case (62 0x448B10, 69 0x448D48, 74 0x448F7E, 76 0x449094, 115 0x44A1A8; 79 0x4492A1 is SX3) reads its operands, calls its starter, **ignores the answer** and goes on with the next line. A script never sees success or failure, never waits for the goal and cannot test it on the same line. | F1 |
| GT155 | 62 perform animation: operands | Operand 0 the first object (0x44B960), operand 1 any value kind, the animation number; the starter is 0x4332E0. A null object or an animation outside 0..25 logs an assertion and answers 0. No start gate, no movement gate, no type test: any object may animate. | F1 |
| GT156 | 62: the frames | **Goal 60** only for a PC or NPC and animation exactly 10 (descriptor 0x5ACDD0, 6 nodes, priority 3, no slot written; answers 1 even if the start fails; a refused build falls through to goal 0). **Goal 0** otherwise (descriptor 0x5A59D0, 6 nodes, priority 3): slot 8 gets the object's art with its animation replaced (0x503E50), which changes the animation only for art types 2, 12 and 13; for any other art the operand is ignored (which kinds those are is inferred). | F1 |
| GT157 | 62: what it replaces | Through the generic starter 0x44D4E0 (GS12): it ends the object's runs of priority 1–3 and is refused by a priority 4/5 run that refuses. No AP charge and no round test: in a round it can start on a critter that is not the current participant. | F1 (GS12) |
| GT158 | 69 walk to: operands | Operand 0 the first object, operands 1 and 2 the values X and Y. The location is X in the low dword and Y in the high dword, **with the high dword Y ORed with X's sign extension**, so a negative X gives Y = −1 (an off-map tile); not classified. It calls 0x4341C0(object, location, 3); the 3 lands in slot 7, which no node reads (GT124). | F1 |
| GT159 | 69: starter, frame, retarget | The movement gate 0x4339A0 first (refused for a null object, one that cannot act, anyone but the current participant in a round, and a non-PC with the reaction slot 0x4C1110 set). Then the finder 0x44E830(object, goal 4; aliases 3, 7, 55). **A match:** the same tile changes nothing; another tile overwrites the bottom frame's destination and sets the replan bit, retargeting in place (goal and gait kept, so a running goal 55 or the hero's click walk is retargeted too). **No match:** goal 7 through 0x44D4E0 (priority 2), the tile in slot 5. A plain walk: run flag 0x40 is never set here. | F1 |
| GT160 | 69/74: what they replace | A new run interrupts at level 2, not the level 3 of GS13: it ends priority 1–2 runs only (fidget, animation loop, other moves); an object with a priority 3+ run (talk, attack, use, animation 0, get-up) refuses the walk and the script never learns it. A retarget happens before any interrupt and touches no other run. | F1 (GS12) |
| GT161 | 74 run to | The same operands and quirk as 69, through 0x434400(object, location, 3). **No match:** goal 55 (priority 2), slot 5 the tile, slot 7 = 3; then, if the encumbrance (0x45F790) is below 4, run flag 0x40 is set on the new run. **A match:** first, if the found run lacks 0x40 and its owner's encumbrance is below 4, it gets 0x40 (a walk becomes a run even for the same tile); then the same tile changes nothing and another tile retargets in place as in 69. The network-host branch is not followed. | F1 |
| GT162 | 76 heal fatigue | Operand 0 an object list (every member), operand 1 a value: fatigue damage = max(damage − value, 0) (a negative value raises it, no upper clamp); if the current fatigue was ≤ 0 before and is > 0 now, get-up 0x434DE0 starts (GT30: gate with cost 0, the art must show animation 7, goal 63 at level 3). A refusal is silent. No script action starts goal 63 directly. | F1 |
| GT163 | 79 use item with skill | Already SX3/SX4: gate 0x4348E0 with cost 4 (no AP charged), goal 40 at priority 3. | F1 (SX3) |
| GT164 | 115 try to steal 100 coins | Thief = the first object of operand 0, victim = the first of operand 1; the victim must be a PC or NPC with a gold handle and at least 100 gold, otherwise nothing happens. Then 0x4350F0(thief, victim, the whole stack, skill 5): gate with cost 4, goal 33 at priority 3, run flag 0x4000 (GT141); how much is taken is decided by goals 33 → 86. The thief's type is not checked. | F1 |
| GT165 | timing | Every start ends in 0x44D520 → 0x44D730: the first frame is an Anim event 5 ms ahead (GS7, GT17). No node runs inside the action; a retarget schedules nothing and is seen at the run's next step through the replan bit. Later lines of the same run act on the table as already changed. | F1 |
| GT166 | the attachment point | None of the six case bodies reads the run's attachment point or the heartbeat state: a heartbeat behaves like any other point. The only filters are the gates above. | F1 |
| GT167 | script 1717 (`arcanum2.dat`, a heartbeat) | The Crash Site agent's heartbeat uses **74 only** (none of 62, 69, 76, 79, 115): with global flag 2323 set it sets the day and night standpoints of the attachee to a literal tile, then runs 74 to that tile. | F1 |

Not read: the nodes of goal 55 (inferred to be goal 7's); the walk's gate meaning of slot 0x4C1110; what the finder does for runs owned by a follower; the animation-type table behind 0x503E50.

## The S14b read of the executable

Read by one Opus reader in ARC-DEV-233 (no second reader yet, so F1) from the retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52`, verified), statically with `llvm-objdump`, reusing the S13b text; nothing was copied or run. Step codes: 0x70000000 + N is "replace by N" (the popped goal's end node runs, the slots stay); every pop or replace clears run flags 0x4, 0x8, 0x10, 0x20 and 0x800; −2 is the stored delay.

| ID | Subject | Finding | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| GT168 | goal 0 `ag_animate`, descriptor 0x5A59D0 | Priority 3, carried, counted, end node 0x42B090. Nodes: 1 0x42CA90 "not playing" (2 / 3); 2 0x42CC80 the step (**pop after −2** / restart after −2); 3 0x426F10 a critter lying in its fall gets up first (4 / push 63); 4 0x426E80 a concealed NPC unconceals first (5 / push 54); 5 0x42CB10 the start, data slot 8 (6 / restart, delay 0); 6 0x433270 (end / end). The run ends by node 2's pop. | F1 |
| GT169 | goal 0 start | The art comes from slot 8. A stoned object gives false; a PC or NPC with critter flags 0xDA & 0x60 (stunned or paralysed) gives false unless the animation is 17..19. Then the art is set, the delay is 1000 / the header's rate (100 ms when the header does not read), flags 0x4 and 0x8 are cleared and 0x10 "playing" set. The frame is not reset by the start itself. | F1 |
| GT170 | goal 0 step | False for a null or stoned object and for a 0x60 critter outside animations 7, 11 and 17..19; animation 14 with field 0xDB flag 0x4 holds. **At the last frame** it clears 0x10 and gives false (the run pops); one frame before the action frame it sets run flag 0x4, which no node of goals 0, 60 or 51 reads. Else one frame forward. | F1 |
| GT171 | timing | First event 5 ms after the script (GT165); nodes 1, 3, 4, 5 run inline, the start's restart has delay 0, so frame 0 → 1 comes at once and the rest each one delay: duration about (frames − 1) × 1000 / rate. | F1 (the delay-0 event: medium) |
| GT172 | goal 0 end node 0x42B090 | Skipped while the map is cleared, for a killed run (0x8000). A PC or NPC is set to animation 0 (5 when sneaking) at frame 0; **any other object is left on its last frame**. | F1 |
| GT173 | art types | Only art types 2, 12 and 13 carry an animation (5 bits, a value of 26 or more logs "Range exceeded" and reads as 0); any other art type ignores the animation of action 62. Which kinds those are is inferred (critter art; human male and female are type 2). | F1 (names inferred) |
| GT174 | animation numbers | The exe holds no name table for 0..25. Meanings its uses show: 0 stand, 5 concealed, 7 fall, 8 get-up, 9 kneel, 10 magic hands, 23 stun; 11 and 17..19 are allowed under a paralysis. | F1 |
| GT175 | goal 60 `ag_animate_kneel_magic_hands`, 0x5ACDD0 | Priority 3, not carried, counted, no end node. Nodes: 1 (2 / 3); 2 0x42E2D0 the step (**replace by 51**, delay 0 / restart after −2); 3 get-up first; 4 unconceal first; 5 0x42E1B0 the start (6 / restart, 0); 6 (end / end). | F1 |
| GT176 | goal 60 start | Sets the art to the kneel (9) at frame 0 **before its guards**, then refuses a stoned object or a critter with flag 0x40 (0x20 alone is allowed, unlike goal 0). Otherwise the delay is the kneel's. A refused start leaves the kneel frame shown (no end node; anomaly, unclassified). | F1 |
| GT177 | goal 60 step | Guards as the start (a refusal replaces the goal by 51). At the last frame of the kneel the art becomes the hands (10) at frame 0 and **the delay is not recomputed** (the hands play at the kneel's rate). At the last frame of the hands: the art becomes the kneel at its last frame and the goal is replaced by 51. The hands play once. | F1 |
| GT178 | goal 51 `ag_animate_reverse`, 0x5ABBD0 | Priority 3, carried, end node 0x42B090. Nodes: 1; 2 0x42E8B0 the reverse step (pop after −2 / restart after −2); 3 0x42E720 the start (refusals as goal 0; the art stays at the kneel's last frame, the delay is recomputed); 4 (end / end). The step goes one frame back each delay; **at frame 0** it clears 0x10 and pops, and the end node stands the critter. Whole sequence: kneel forward, hands once, kneel backward, stand. | F1 |
| GT179 | object state touched | Only the art id (field 1) and run flags 0x4, 0x8, 0x10; the flags read are 0x14 bit 0x10000, critter 0xDA 0x20 / 0x40, 0xDB 0x4 (animation 14) and 0x13 bit 0x1 (a destroyed object never advances). No rotation, sound or effects. | F1 |
| GT180 | round and action points | No node reads the round flag. Action points are charged only on the refused-start path (node 6 / 4 charges 2 of the current participant, and the stepper's end step charges the global small cost); the normal end is a pop and charges nothing. All three goals are counted, so while one runs the subturn callback waits (GT25) and the NPC's AI tick stops (GT35). | F1 |
| GT181 | dead or destroyed objects | No node tests life: a dead critter can be animated (a body showing animation 7 first pushes get-up, whose start refuses), and goal 0's end node then sets animation 0 (anomaly, unclassified). | F1 (partial) |
| GT182 | edge cases | A frame at or past the new animation's count wraps to 0. Art types 0, 1 and 6 have no frame field, so the run ends at the first step when the header count is 1, otherwise it restarts every delay until interrupted (inferred). | F1 |

Not read: the animation names (not in the exe); 0x430490; the exact pose test of 0x45D730; the destroy path; whether the Anim-event clock is real or game time (GS7).

## The S14d read of the executable

Read by one Opus reader in ARC-DEV-235 (no second reader yet, so F1) from the retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52`, verified), statically with `llvm-objdump`; nothing was copied or run. The action table has 141 entries (jump table 0x44ADBC, entry = type − 1); every case below goes on to the next line whatever its starter answers (GT154). **What this read adds to GS30:** the casts start a goal only through the spell activation and only with a parent (61, 136 and a null caster start none), no script action starts dying except through the damage path (58, 60, 138), 93 become prone starts no goal, 36 attack starts goal 53 or 0, and 48 is *cast spell* (the "set area known" of SS3 is a different numbering).

| ID | Subject | Finding | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| GT183 | the cast actions | **48** cast spell (caster object, spell value, target object); **61** cast spell on (spell, target; no caster); **118** cast free spell (= 48 with invocation flag 0x2); **121** cast unresistable (= 48 with flag 0x4); **122** free and unresistable (flags 0x6); **136** cast unresistable spell (= 61 with flag 0x4). Each fills an invocation (init 0x455A20 with the source or none and the spell; the target through 0x4440E0; flags at +0xDC) and calls the dispatcher 0x455AC0. Flag 0x2 becomes spell-run flag 0x2 (free), flag 0x4 run flag 0x10 (unresistable, RN3). The case has no starter, gate or type test. | F1 |
| GT184 | which goal a cast starts | Only through the activation 0x455C30 (RN4, SU13, QC1). With a parent (the caster, 0x450A50): goal 26 when the spell record +0x68 is −1, else goal 82, built by 0x44D4E0; with goal 82 a second goal 84 is built by 0x44D500 (no interrupt). A running 26/27 makes the new goal queue under it (0x44DBE0); at a stack depth of 4 or more, or in a round, the cast is refused and the run freed (RN4). Goal 73 needs invocation flag 0x1, which no script action sets. A refused build or start frees the spell run. **61, 136 and 48/118/121/122 with a null caster have no parent, so no goal starts:** the run is processed by a magic-tech event 2 ms later. The only gates are magic switched off and the invocation check 0x4564E0. | F1 (RN2–RN4) |
| GT185 | 58 kill | Operand 0 is an object list; each non-null member goes to 0x45D900: a combat context with **no attacker**, damage flag 0x4 (damage = the victim's current hit points), the damage apply 0x4B4390, which calls the kill 0x45DA20 (killer none, animation **7** with flag 0x4) when the victim reads dead; the kill starts **goal 31** through 0x435080. Afterwards the hit-point damage is written as 32000. Only under a module flag 0x5E863C (inferred: the editor) is the art put straight to animation 7 at its last frame. | F1 (the flag: medium) |
| GT186 | who starts goal 31 | 0x435080 has one caller (0x45DA20), which has one caller (0x4B4390): so only **58 kill**, **60 damage** (object list, amount, damage type; lethal damage over 20 gives animation 18, otherwise 7) and **138 apply unresistable damage** (as 60 with context flag 0x1000000) can start dying; the killer is none in all three. 60 and 138 also stun, knock down and bust at level 5. | F1 |
| GT187 | 93 become prone | Operand 0 an object list; each member goes to 0x435B30: **0x424070(obj, level 4, quiet 0, local 0)**; a refusal does nothing; otherwise the art becomes **animation 7 at its last frame**. It starts **no goal** and changes no critter flag, combat mode, focus, danger state or follower. No type test. The object lies in the fall pose with no run until something starts get-up. | F1 |
| GT188 | 109 stop attacking | Operand 0 one object, to 0x4AA4A0. **An NPC with a focus (field 0x11B):** peace 0x4AA300 with that focus (clears 0x11B and 0x11C, raises the reaction to a PC head to 50, the attack-goal interrupts, the other side's followers), then **0x424070(npc, level 3)**, then leaves combat mode 0x4B3F20 (whose 0x4B3F80 can interrupt at level 3 too); with a leader (0x45DDA0) NPC flags 0x118 gets 0x20000000 (no attack). Always the target lock (bit 0x200000 of critter flags 0xDB) is cleared. **A PC:** the same for each follower. An NPC with no focus is not interrupted. | F1 |
| GT189 | eye candy actions | **120** play script eye candy (entry value, object) → 0x44C7A0 → the add 0x4CCF70 (GT70): no interrupt, goals 45–48, 58, 59, 75, 76 by entry flags, or a static overlay; refuses a null or destroyed object. **124** stop → remove 0x4CD940. **104** play spell eye candy → 0x456E60, **116** stop → 0x456EC0. | F1 |
| GT190 | other actions that start goals | **36** attack: the attack event 0x4A9650, for an NPC victim 0x433440 starts goal 53 or 0 (not read further). **75** heal hp: get-up when fatigue > 0, ends an NPC's flee 25, calls the fatigue setter. **130** stop spell: the spell-run processing ends AGoal components. | F1 (36: low) |
| GT191 | knock-down through the fatigue setter | 0x45D5A0 starts **goal 62** through 0x435A90 (level-5 interrupt) when a PC or NPC goes from able to unable to act, unless it already shows animation 7 or is dead; reached from 76 with a negative amount, 40, 75 and 77 add effect. | F1 |
| GT192 | actions that only interrupt | **22** disband and **21** critter follow when it first leaves an existing leader (the leave 0x45DFC0 → 0x45E040: level 5 on the follower; a join interrupts nothing, C5; 21 also copies the leader's concealment), **67** wait for leader, **68** destroy and **126** destroy item named (for a destroyed follower), **23** float line, **24** print line and **86** float newspaper headline (can-speak 0x4AD800 → level 2 on the NPC). | F1 |
| GT193 | completeness | Every case was swept for calls reaching the goal builders or the interrupt functions within three calls; nothing else matches within that depth. Not read (four calls deep): 7 call script, 25/27, 35, 56/57, 59, 117/128/137, 133, 135, 112. | F1 |
| GT194 | 60 damage, 138 apply unresistable damage | Read in ARC-DEV-243 (one Opus reader, F1). Operand 0 an object list (every member), operand 1 the amount, operand 2 the damage type; each member gets a fresh combat context (0x4B2210) with **no attacker**, the amount written at the damage array slot of the type (0 normal, 1 poison, 2 electrical, 3 fire, 4 fatigue; the type is not range-checked, a type of 5 or more or −1 overwrites other context fields) and goes to the damage apply 0x4B4390. 138 differs from 60 only by context flag 0x1000000, which makes the **resistance step 0x4B6860 return at once**; nothing else. The case goes on to the next line (GT154). | F1 |
| GT195 | the damage apply for a script | Steps that apply to a script's damage: a null target does nothing; poison is zeroed for a critter with flags 0x20000004 and everything for an invulnerable object; the target's script at attachment point 0x17 can cancel the damage (re-entry guarded); object flags 0x400001 stop it; a critter with bit 0x200 of field 0xDB takes nothing from a script (no weapon in the context); a wall is skipped; resistance subtracts damage × resistance / 100 per type (fatigue uses three quarters of the damage resistance); hit points = normal + electrical + fire, poison and fatigue separate; **difficulty scaling is for a PC attacker only, so never for a script; there is no armour or armour-class step; the critical multipliers and effects (stun, knock-down, blinding...) need damage flags a script never sets**. A non-critter (portal, container) loses a material value and is destroyed at 0 hit points (trap check first). A critter: fire damage wears the torso armour (+10), the total is added to the hit-point damage (a negative amount heals a critter, not an object), a follower falling below 20 % notifies its leader, the fatigue setter 0x45D5A0 runs with fatigue damage plus the fatigue-type amount (a **knock-down (goal 62) comes only from there**, GT191), poison is added to the poison stat, a dead critter is skipped; a death runs the kill 0x45DA20 with no killer, animation 18 when the total after resistance is strictly over 20 (flag 0x4 clear), else 7; an NPC left alive gets an immediate AI pass 0x4A84F0 with no attacker (its goals not read). Floaters show only under floater setting 2. This **corrects GT186**: stun, knock-down and bust at level 5 belong to combat crits, not to scripts. | F1 |
| GT196 | 75 heal hit points | Operand 0 an object list, operand 1 the amount; the same fresh context with the amount in the normal slot, the heal 0x4B58C0: only a PC or NPC, a dead critter does nothing (resurrection needs flag 0x2, which 75 does not set), a living critter's hit-point damage falls by the amount and is clamped at 0 (nothing for an amount of 0 or less or no damage); then the follower UI refresh and the reaction-mood update 0x4B80E0. An unconscious critter is healed but does not get up. **75 starts no get-up (63) and no flee end**: the get-up 0x434DE0, the fatigue setter and the resurrect and flee-end calls are in the heal function's fatigue and resurrect branches, which 75 never reaches (this **corrects GT190**). | F1 |
| GT197 | what S14g did not read | The goals of the NPC AI pass 0x4A84F0 with no attacker after a surviving damage; 0x459A20 (the spell-flag 0x40000 hook); 0x43F1C0 (object destroy) in detail; whether 0x43D6D0 caps a resistance; the multiplayer branches. | F1 |
| GT198 | the point-0x17 call in the damage apply | Read in ARC-DEV-254 (one Opus reader, F1). 0x4B4390 calls the object script runner 0x441980 (the runner every point uses) once, at 0x4B446B: triggerer = the context's attacker (null handle when there is none: a script's own damage runs it all the same), attachee = the target, extra object = null, point 0x17, line 0. The script comes after the network gate, the null-target exit and the critter poison/zero step, and **before** the destroyed/invulnerable gate (0x400001), the critter field-0xDB gate, the wall exit and the resistance step, so it runs for destroyed or invulnerable objects, walls, dead critters and every object type. | F1 |
| GT199 | veto | The runner answers 0 for "skip default" and nonzero for "run default"; a zero ends 0x4B4390 at its exit at once: no resistance, no hit-point or fatigue change, no floater or log text, no bust, no attack notice, no worn-item scripts. The steps before the script stay applied but only for a critter: the poison zeroing (critter flags 0x20000004) and the zeroing of all amounts (field 0x14 bit 0x10000). No script (number 0), or the editor global, runs default without a run; a script file that cannot be opened and a native trap script skip the default; a script that runs past its last line skips the default (F0). | F1 (the last: F0) |
| GT200 | the re-entry guard | Global 0x5FC26C, a flag set to 1 right before the script call and to 0 right after it (it brackets the script call only, not the whole step; initial 0, three references, all in 0x4B4390). A damage dealt while it is 1 (any damage a point-0x17 script's actions deal, including 60/138) skips the script and goes on to the gates with no veto possible. | F1 |
| GT201 | other runners | None: of the 72 direct calls of 0x441980 only 0x4B446B passes 0x17. **Correction of the script-trigger audit:** 0x4B47F8 is not a point-23 site; its register is 0x22 (34) or 0x23 (35) by context flag 0x2, one run per worn type-7 item while context flag 0x4 is set (triggerer the attacker, attachee the item, extra the target). Call-ex (0x44B1D6) takes its point from the operand, so a script may call point 23 directly; that run passes neither the guard nor the veto. | F1 |
| GT202 | what the script can change | The invocation holds only slot, line, triggerer, attachee, extra and point; 0x4B4390 publishes no pointer to the context. A script cannot read or change the amounts, only veto; it can change object state the later gates re-read (object flags, critter field 0xDB, resistances, hit points), but not the poison/zero step's inputs (read before it). | F1 |
| GT203 | what ARC-DEV-254 did not read | The interpreter's condition and action tables for any action that reaches the damage context; whether callers of 0x4B4390 read the context after a veto; the meaning of field 0x14 bit 0x10000, context flags 0x2/0x4 and 0x45D8D0 ("dead") are inferred. | F0 |
| GT204 | the NPC pick pocket attempt | Read in ARC-DEV-246 (one Opus reader, F1). Goal 86 node 4 (0x42A2A0) treats a PC and an NPC thief alike: it refuses a missing thief or victim and a victim with object flags 0x3 (destroyed or off), charges the thief 4 action points (0x4B7CD0; an answer of 0 skips the check), fills the invocation (0x4C7090: source, target, item, flags, skill from the frame, modifier; frame flag +0x70 bit 1, the plant, sets invocation flag 0x2) and calls 0x4C7160, whose result bit 0x01 sets run flag 0x40000; the node returns 1 even when the check fails. 0x4C7160 dispatches skill − 1 through its tables: skill 5 goes to the steal case (0x4C743C) and, with flag 0x2, to the plant (0x4C7735); nothing tells a PC thief from an NPC one or a PC victim from an NPC one except the fate point (0x4C72E0, thief type 15 only) and the interface callbacks (0x4C763D steal, 0x4C795A plant). | F1 |
| GT205 | who starts an NPC's pick pocket | The starter 0x4350F0 has 7 direct callers: the four window wrappers (the PC's), script action 115 (0x44A25F: the item is the victim's gold pile, field 0xE8, at least 100 coins, flag 0), the dialog's "use skill on" response (0x414B37 → 0x419CB0, whose item chooser 0x4C91F0 picks an item only for skills 10 and 14, so a dialog-started skill 5 carries a null item and the case fails unnoticed) and the AI action executor 0x4AC350 (action kind 3), whose only writer 0x4A8BD0 hard-codes skill 10 (heal). **The NPC AI never starts a pick pocket on its own**; in practice an NPC thief comes from script action 115. Whether a dialog line requests skill 5 is data (not read). | F1 (the dialog's data F0) |
| GT206 | the check | Pick pocket's skill flags are 0x998 (table 0x5B6F64 entry 5): perception-opposed 0x10, awareness 0x08, eyes 0x80, arms 0x100, cap 95 (0x800): no distance and no light term. Effectiveness (0x4C62E0 → the opposed branch 0x4C6410): the thief's raw level minus the victim's effective perception through the SC4 table, +10 for extraordinary intelligence, cap 95; the difficulty-setting adjustment (0x40DA50, 0x404590) applies to the local PC only. Difficulty: the modifier plus 0x4C8430 (SC5–SC11: the awareness terms, blinded thief +30, crippled arms +20/+50, the worn slot table, 5 × width × height, halved from expert). The roll: success when random(1, 100) + difficulty ≤ effectiveness; a second random(1, 100) decides a critical (0x4C81A0 on a success, 0x4C82E0 on a failure); result bits 0x01 success, 0x10 critical. An NPC thief differs from the hero's model (`PickPocketRules`) only by having no fate point and no difficulty-setting term. | F1 |
| GT207 | what a success transfers | The case first requires the item's holder (0x4612A0) to be the victim (for a plant, the thief), else the failure is never noticed. Gold (item type 8) moves min(quantity, 100) through 0x464830(victim, thief, n, pile): the victim's pile is debited (destroyed when emptied, 0x43CCA0), the thief's pile (field 0xE8) grows or a new one is made and the receiver's insert-item script (point 21) runs; ammunition min(quantity, 20) (0x4658E0); any other item whole (0x4617F0). The window functions 0x4C6FD0 and 0x4C7010 are only wrappers of the starter and are not used for an NPC thief. After a steal that moved something: 0x45DC90(thief, victim, 0) lowers the thief's alignment (PP5; **no PC type test, so an NPC thief too**) and the victim's critter flags 2 (field 0xDB) get bit 0x01 (PP6). For an NPC thief no message, floater, experience, skill-use tracking or reaction write comes from the case (the messages 500/501 are the PC-only callbacks). | F1 |
| GT208 | a failure, and being noticed | Detection only on a failure: a critical failure is always noticed; otherwise only an untrained thief (training 0), when the victim's perception roll 0x45F060(victim, 5, 0) succeeds (random(1, 20) ≤ perception); a master planting never. When noticed, in order: the thief's script at point 33 (caught thief; its answer ignored), the victim's script at point 11 (catching thief; any answer but run default stops there), an NPC victim (type 16) whose leader (0x45DE00) is the thief stops, else the attack event 0x4A9650(thief, victim, 1, 0) (call sites 0x4C7728 steal, 0x4C793B plant); a PC victim never takes the leader test. Goal 86's end: on success node 5 (0x42A430) reads 0x40000 and node 8 plays animation 0; on failure animation 12, the wait 0x42CB10, then node 9 (0x433270) charges 2 more action points to anyone but the local PC (an NPC pays 6 in all); a refused charge makes node 4 return 0, the goal's failure, with nothing rolled. | F1 |
| GT209 | caps, and what ARC-DEV-246 did not read | Script action 115 needs at least 100 coins; a success moves at most 100 (or 20 ammunition). Neither the nodes nor the case test "no pickpocket" (critter flags 2 bit 0x80) or the mechanical flag (0xDA bit 0x20000000), which only the skill bar tests, so a script thief can rob such a victim; nothing refuses a dead, unconscious or stoned victim (an unconscious or dead one lowers the difficulty by 50 through the awareness term, F0). A multiplayer client returns 0. **Unread:** whether a dialog response passes skill 5; the effects of 0x4A9650 on a victim beyond GT140; an inventory redraw or sound for a PC victim's gold change inside 0x43CCA0 / 0x4EFDD0 / 0x4605D0; whether the sight and hearing tests 0x4AF260 / 0x4AF470 depend on the victim's facing; the action-point charge outside a round (0x4B7CD0, CT4). | F1 (F0 where marked) |
